# Android gameplay anomaly ledger

## Continue player vision — 2026-10-27

Confirmed Revamped restore defect: native fresh idle vision has an active root at scale (1,1,1); a fresh-process Continue of the same checkpoint leaves that active root at (0,0,1). Its child red/blue sprites, layers and VisionRange value remain correct. The restore state replaces normal opening animation flow without completing its root-scale initialization. Native runs13/14 reproduce the visible idle-darkness difference and fail the exact scale contract. Restore now sets that presentation root to unit scale before entering CombatState, leaving the stat-driven children and saved upgrades untouched. No checkpoint replacement, per-frame override or artificial light is added. Verification includes an actually acquired Night Vision upgrade.

## Exploration chest fog visibility — 2026-10-27

Confirmed Revamped presentation defect: ExplorationRun.Create copied the normal closed-chest sprite, sorting layer and order but omitted its FogCircleRed and FogCircleBlue children. Native inspection finds those children on layer 8 with unchanged red/blue channel colors, T_Circle sprite and scales 1 / 1.5; the world Arrow uses the separate visible-in-fog layer 9. Source inspection confirms no lifetime/expiry exists for exploration drops. Their pointer hid based on camera position while the artwork remained shadowed. Native POI run-2 reproduces the missing reveal children and visibly contrasts a bright boss chest with the obscured exploration chest.

The correction restores the exact two native reveal children. The separately requested reward glow uses native circle sprites on the existing visible-in-fog layer and fades by actual player distance, without recoloring the red/blue mask channels, changing reward logic or adding a lifetime. The first diagnostic run's pointer assertion was a batch-render setup error; the actual FogOfWarCamera must render its own mask texture before a windowless screenshot. Run-4 also exposed a fixture assumption: in-view targets may never have needed an edge marker, so an absent marker is legitimate. Its assertion now accepts either an absent or hidden marker, preserving the actual visibility requirement. Current source/integration and release gates are tracked in PROGRESS.md.

Run-8 was cancelled through the validation player's cancellation signal after the newly added presentation frames let a restored native boss chest open before the fixture moved away. The old checkpoint legitimately includes that boss reward. The correction restores the fixture's original ordering: move away immediately, then inspect the exploration presentation. The checkpoint and production behavior remain unchanged.

## Phoenix visible-angle synchronization — 2026-10-27

Confirmed presentation defect: the retained native Projectile angle setter writes Rigidbody2D.rotation, while visible Transform rotation can remain at the old angle until a physics update. Native orientation run-2 reproduces this before the correction, including pooled reuse; the first diagnostic capture shows all eight requested directions facing right. A postfix on that existing setter publishes the same angle immediately to Transform.rotation, with no per-frame correction or particle/artwork rewrite. Run-6 verifies eight directions in both visual styles and two activations for every one of 37 loaded Projectile templates, preserving body angle, velocity and authored child poses. It also verifies the separate SpiritSummon/PF_Icicle movement-facing path, inventories five shooting/orbital/stabbing summon owners, exercises actual Scarlett burn and Fire Starter damage contacts, and preserves Yuki's moth animation after a normal scene restart. This is a bounded same-cause audit, not a claim that every possible animation or combat build was played.

Diagnostics run-4 lacked the damage-kind observer required by the reused emitter contact assertion; run-5 reached the restart test with Yuki locked in the test profile. Both were fixture defects corrected without another production change. Run-6 completes the whole correction/audit. Current source and render hashes, scope and closure review are retained in L1-acceptance.json; final public delivery is recorded in PROGRESS.md. This correction does not alter the Android parity classification or any combat recipe.

Final screenshot review of the first copied package caught an empty Reborn capture despite passing angle assertions. Original particle data explains it: wings emit 40 particles per travelled unit, tail 20, and both emit zero per second. The fixture had disabled movement and attempted time-only simulation. Run-7 correctly fails the added nonempty-buffer assertion. Run-8 uses actual native movement and waits for nonempty particle buffers, without manual emission or altered particle modules; both inspected styles and the complete orientation/contact/restart audit pass. Production code is unchanged by this capture correction. The final committed package repeats this gate before publication.

## Reported controller selector defects — 2026-10-25

| Finding | Classification and reproduced cause | Correction / acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| A296 — blank initial character preview | Existing Windows menu defect. Native `Menu.Show` calls `Button.Select` before installing its selection listeners; the first controller focus therefore leaves the serialized placeholder name/description and base portrait. Reproduced without moving the mouse in S1/run-26. | Initialize character/weapon preview through the native selection event after Show finishes wiring. Actual first entry, remembered Bulwark and re-entry pass in S1/run-28/30 at720p/1080p. |
| A297 — unreachable appended roster entries | Port integration defect. Stock buttons use explicit navigation. Charge Cannon copied the template's outgoing links, while no original slot linked to it; real controller right/down ends on Spore Gun. The added Gale/Bulwark slots have the same missing incoming links. S1/run-26 records the actual graph and input failure. | Extend existing grid links and restore valid appended remembered indices. Controller visits all15 characters/12 weapons; pointer raycast/hover/click and locked/obtained confirmation pass in S1/run-28/30. The stock gun menu's persistence flag remains false; no new preference policy is invented. |

These are menu defects, not Android gameplay/balance discrepancies. No gameplay formulas, earned ownership, credits or user saves are reset to correct them.

This is the maintained anomaly ledger for Android **8.6 / versionCode 1093**, compared with Windows Steam build **12679152**. Stable A-numbers below identify findings across the README, source research and implementation records. Recording an anomaly does not defer its implementation: reachable Android gameplay behavior remains the parity target, including apparent bugs. Independent balance corrections require a separate user instruction.

Each finding records the observed behavior and gameplay consequence, source evidence and its limits, and current implementation/verification disposition. Static decoding is distinguished from runtime reproduction. An unresolved consumer or reachability question remains open; an implemented-but-unverified change is not completed parity. New findings belong here as they are detected, using a new stable ID or an explicit correction to an existing one.

## Classification review before build and game validation

User instruction, 2026-10-22: review this ledger to distinguish Android bugs from gameplay refinements **before any build or game validation**. This review belongs to the existing ACTIVE P2 stage. It does not authorize balance corrections or a second implementation stage. Android gameplay, including its bugs, remains the parity target unless the user explicitly changes that requirement.

Anger Point actual hurt refresh/30-second expiry and source-unsaved phase omission pass. These extend existing findings; no Android balance/bug correction or new anomaly mechanism is introduced. Other derived buff families, legacy/combined and final delivery acceptance remain open.

These outcomes extend existing findings without adding an anomaly mechanism or independently correcting Android behavior; other ranks/repeated/legacy/Custom, actors/modes/UI and final delivery remain open.

Classification and delivery status are independent. The table below classifies the currently recorded findings; the detailed entries retain their evidence, reachability limits and implementation/verification status. A source-confirmed calculation is not automatically a confirmed Android runtime symptom, and neither establishes the developer's intent. New findings must receive a classification before the pre-build review can close. The review through A272 is **REVIEWED**, as recorded below. Newly discovered source findings must be classified before their affected acceptance checks; classification never authorizes an independent behavior correction.

The first whole-port build/game validation may begin only after the remaining source implementation is finished and this review records all of the following:

- Every recorded finding has a classification, evidence boundary and parity disposition; newly discovered findings are included.
- Likely bugs are distinguished from numerical/gameplay changes, text mismatches, implementation quirks, Windows defects and port/research errors. Related consumers of one bug are grouped without inflating the mechanism count.
- Unresolved facets distinguish unknown intent or an unobserved symptom from missing required behavior or reachability. Required source/consumer/checkpoint gaps are resolved; unknown intent alone does not justify withholding established Android behavior.
- The final source batch follows Android behavior, including classified bugs, except for the user's explicit overrides. No independent fix, invented callback, guard or other behavioral change is justified by classification alone.
- The reviewed source revision and any uncommitted patch state are identified. The review records only source reconciliation; compilation and game behavior remain unverified until the subsequent whole-port checks run.

Current gate: **Classification REVIEWED through A280 before affected compilation/runtime acceptance.** A276–A279 are required Android gameplay integrations and Windows port omissions; A280 restores the exact source startup state operation. Complete affected workflows pass S4/400/402/403–405. P2 remains ACTIVE for bounded further discovery and final reconciliation/delivery. No new final release or exhaustive parity claim.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| G | Gameplay refinement / tuning: an evidenced rule, content, geometry, cadence or numerical change. This does not claim it is beneficial or deliberately designed. |
| B | Likely Android bug: a contract contradiction, conditional failure or inconsistent state lifecycle is evidenced. The reason and any unproven runtime precondition are stated; developer intent is not asserted. |
| T | Text/behavior mismatch: a description disagrees with shipped data or execution. This establishes a content discrepancy, not permission to change the mechanic. |
| Q | Implementation quirk: source behavior is established, but the evidence does not justify calling it a defect or a refinement. |
| U | Unresolved: producer, reachability, combined outcome or balancing claim is insufficiently established. |
| W | Windows defect / Android correction: the defect belongs to the original Windows build, not an Android regression. |
| I | Integration/research issue: an adapter, checkpoint, fixture or source-reading problem; not an Android gameplay bug. |
| N | No defect established: an interpretation was corrected, behavior matches at the stated boundary, or content is dormant. |

Multiple codes split the facets of a finding rather than classifying a whole subsystem as faulty. The B entries are candidates for a separately authorized balance/bug patch; they remain required parity behavior now. U entries remain source/reachability work, not permission to invent a missing publisher or consumer. Historical Windows corrections do not validate the rest of the current compiled batch.

### Recorded-finding review — 2026-10-23

Reviewed the classification table against the detailed findings through A273. There are **273 records**, with overlapping facets: 158 G, 26 B, 6 T, 105 Q, 36 U, 21 W, 115 I and 31 N. These are neither feature-completion counts nor independent bug counts. A24/A79/A135 describe one Smite producer defect and its consumers; A143/A256 describe one saved-buff-count/lifecycle mismatch. That leaves **23 distinct likely-bug mechanisms** among the 26 B-labelled records. A136-A259 extend the recorded-finding review during source implementation; The final pre-build classification review is now recorded separately above; runtime and delivery acceptance remain open.

| Likely-bug family | Why it differs from ordinary tuning | Evidence limit / parity disposition |
|---|---|---|
| A221 — non-saving unlock state | IsUnlock/IsGet return _isLocked, set by Lock and cleared by Unlock, inverting ownership when isSave=false. | All27 original entries use isSave=true; no shipped-instance symptom is established. Native M10 ownership is explicitly retained; no correction or unused branch transplant is added. |
| A43 — Shatter | Unfrozen deaths trigger an effect described as frozen-death-only. | Source dispatch is established; retain the missing freeze restriction. The changed pool boundary remains unverified. |
| A58 — Elemental Barrage | Fire Rate is modified twice while the description promises Fire Rate and Reload Rate. | Shared Windows/Android bug, not an Android-only regression; retain both Fire Rate operations. |
| A73 — Might | A positive addition to negative HP change reduces damage rather than increasing it. | Signed arithmetic established; preserve it together with A132's single asset callback. No developer-intent claim. |
| A76 — positional lightning | A reached attack supplies a positive HP change to enemies. | Preserve that change and the separate retained collision damage; their combined outcome needs whole-port validation. |
| A24/A79/A135 — Smite consumers | A nominal kill notification is emitted for surviving/protected captured targets. | One producer issue affects Justice, Sword and Shield, and Angelic; retain each consumer's source counting. |
| A87 — pool maximum | A named maximum is exceeded by one because rejection uses strictly greater-than. | Preserve 51/51/36 for the recorded 50/50/35 configurations; not an enemy-count cap. |
| A89 — hidden pooled renderer | The inspected reuse path does not restore a renderer disabled by visibility eviction. | Preserve the source state transitions; an Android-device visibility reproduction is not established here. |
| A95 — offer exhaustion | Unique-choice selection cannot finish when fewer eligible entries remain than requested. | Conditional algorithmic failure established; natural Android exhaustion is unresolved. No count clamp or escape is added. |
| A97 — Specialize lifetime | Spent exclusion state resets differently on teardown and checkpoint restore. | Preserve the distinct source paths. Legacy destroyed-helper reconstruction is a separate port issue; its local mapping is unverified. |
| A100 — Bulwark asset lifetime | Perk fields survive passive reset and affect later runs/replay. | Original asset retention is evidenced; arbitrary dynamic unloading is outside that proof. No per-run repair is added. |
| A122 — arena edge release | Wind Shield can deactivate a cached edge which geometry updates do not reactivate. | Conditional contact consequence; Android-device contact not reproduced. Preserve the source release/update paths. |
| A125 — late-run damage update | Configured logic resides in a ScriptableObject Update without an identified caller/binding. | Likely inert at the inspected lifecycle boundary; retain U until required reachability review closes. Do not supply a new coroutine. |
| A138 — pulse configuration without listeners | Enabling the perk does not itself store its configuration, so a later summon can remain unconfigured. | Conditional source failure, not an Android device reproduction. Preserve listener-driven data ownership without a direct configuration assignment in Apply. |
| A143/A256 — buff count differs from active contribution/lifecycle | Applications increment saved count while removals do not decrement it. The source dispatch includes temporary/nonstacking subclasses but omits their timer/coroutine/active flags. | Preserve counter replay and fresh unsaved lifecycle, including the three inherited roots. Actual Android and Windows continuation symptoms remain unverified; raw aggregate restoration or an invented phase/flag is not parity proof. |
| A158 — Growth reward on restored level | Restoring XP emits the same level notification as advancement, and Growth tests only the level modulo five. | A selected, enabled original Growth rune can schedule its heart-drop coroutine again at a qualifying saved level. Source producer/binding/callback established; Android-device drop/collection not reproduced. Do not suppress the callback. |
| A181 — reused burn tick phase | Release clears payload/target but leaves partial elapsed time; Affect also leaves it. | Preserve source free-stack reuse across targets; actual carried-tick gameplay outcome remains unverified. |

| A265 — Hidden Blade knockback collision exclusion | Source layer9 excludes Monster12 and VisibleInFogMonster15 despite an activated radius1.7/force4 child. | Original source matrix/hierarchy and native ignored9/13/zero callbacks agree; preserve the shipped exclusion. Android runtime and reassigned layers are not claimed. |

Ordinary numerical, geometry, cadence and composition changes remain G even when they weaken a build: examples include Culling, Mini Clip, Tome penalties, Giant, Dragon/Scythe, freeze durations and map schedules. G does not assert good balance or author intent. Text discrepancies remain separately identified. A49's loss of HP scaling has an established source formula but unknown intent; its sign defect is already A73, so it is not counted as another bug.

The U facets are bounded in each classified finding; the following earlier review notes remain supplemented by the later entries:

- A06/A09: complete the required consumer/ambiguous-instance coverage; matching names or fields do not close it.
- A07: the community balance complaint has no measured scenario/build result; do not infer an Android fix from it.
- A14: reconcile live global-player summon lookup and teardown ownership; the old Windows guard is not Android lifetime proof.
- A28/A123 and A52: investigate the named notification/keyed-provider consumers; do not invent a publisher or cooldown effect.
- A49: intent is unknown; implementation follows the established constant formula without waiting for intent evidence.
- A91: end-of-frame startup reload delivery is established (A141); receiver creation/Start order must still establish what the skipped callback means in play.
- A95/A122/A125: retain the conditional/lifecycle limits stated in the bug-family table; source findings are not Android runtime reproductions.
- A126: finish required legacy typed-listener/publisher reachability coverage while retaining the established direct death order.
- A131: the shipped direct-grant recipe is established; any claim of reachable timed conversion still needs a demonstrated producer.
- A138: distinguish the established no-listener condition from actual acquisition/Start order. Trainer requires a summon-related predecessor; Dragon's original object is enabled, and its egg disables it in Start, so an unhatched egg does not by itself prove the absence of a listener.
- A143: the selected Generator count/contribution/start bridge and released native-provider conversion are implemented locally, unverified. Resolve the remaining source runtime/stat-owner reconciliation and verify actual continuation ordering/outcome; do not normalize the source count to one active bonus.
- A144: retain the absent automatic cleanup; a direct-call scan does not settle arbitrary manual/indirect invocation or demonstrate a persistent live-stat symptom.
- A146: only a compiled class is established. Any claim of a reached ManualReloadState needs a demonstrated producer/type binding rather than its suggestive name.
- A149: fresh schema7 implements source listener/accumulator/counter and per-powerup additive replay locally. Older aggregate checkpoints still need source-semantic reconciliation, and actual callback/start/continuation acceptance remains open. Unlimited Ritual effects did not record total accepted actions; A239 rebuilds without their unattributed aggregate bonus. Recorded source accumulators remain additive replay inputs.
- A153: fresh schema7 implements Feed's source accumulator/additive restore within its powerup group. Actual continuation remains unverified. Legacy unlimited native effects do not reveal their historical grant total; A239 rebuilds without their unattributed aggregate damage. Known source accumulators remain additive replay inputs.
- A155: the original source buff/helper/event recipes are established and reconstructed locally. Old unlimited Berserk/Bulk Up actions lack historical grant counts; their known aggregate bonuses remain preserved. Actual helper counts, callbacks and continuation are unverified.
- A156: schema7 implements the selected source runtime ordering/additive calls without aggregate-stat substitution. Legacy aggregate reconstruction and other remaining source consumers still require reconciliation, and no current/legacy continuation behavior is validated by this source edit.
- A158: distinguish the source-confirmed restored-level callback and Growth drop scheduling from actual device pickup/collection. Pool/visual conditions and a natural Android continuation have not been reproduced. Unknown intent does not justify suppressing the reached callback.

This review changes documentation only. No Android bug is corrected, no parity behavior is postponed for balance approval, and no build or game validation has run. The **final pre-build review remains open** because required source coverage/implementation and new findings are still possible. Before the first build, reconcile every newly added finding and the remaining U acceptance work against the final source batch. Unknown intent alone does not block importing established behavior; unresolved required behavior or reachability does block acceptance. Then perform the user's requested whole-port build/game validation.

## Scope correction — 2026-10-22

The user requires Android gameplay changes except monetization. Earlier entries describing balance/mechanics as optional or directing Windows behavior to be retained record an unauthorized implementer restriction; those dispositions are superseded. Their source observations remain evidence. All reachable gameplay differences require migration and verification, including numeric tuning, cooldowns, stacking/refresh and map/Endless rules. Apparent Android source anomalies require reachability/behavior investigation; they are not grounds for silently preferring Windows. Explicit user progression/HUD/Custom requirements and preservation/rollback contracts remain. This ledger is not a claim of completed parity.

## Current source findings — 2026-10-22 implementation batch

These entries record observed Android behavior, not proposed balance fixes or postponed implementation decisions. The current batch is uncompiled and unverified. As requested, acceptance validation waits until the whole port is implemented and integrated. ARM64/static-asset evidence below is not an Android runtime test.

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |

| A87 — pool cap is exceeded by one | `IsMax` rejects a fetch only when the existing active count is **greater than** the configured maximum. Shatter's maximum 50 therefore permits 51 simultaneous actors; the next fetch is rejected. The same comparison applies to other pools. BurnFX registers 50 and each freeze-effect pool 35, permitting 51/36 visuals respectively. These are not limits on the number of affected enemies. | `ObjectPooler.IsMax`0x1967e58 ends in `cmp`/`cset gt`; `GetPooledObject`0x19647dc checks it before acquisition. Explicit registrations: `IceShatterOnDeath.Start`0x1899c90, `BurnSystem.Start` 0x196102c, `FreezeSystem.Start` 0x1972bf0. Private evidence: global-pool-source and dynamic-pool-registration-source ARM exports. | Strict greater-than is implemented locally in the shared pool. The earlier 50-active Shatter fixture is historical evidence for the previous implementation, not proof of this corrected source limit. No clamp to 50 is retained. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A88 — warm-up count does not mean that many allocated objects | The scene requests 6000 SmallXP warm-up iterations, but `AddObject` immediately releases each fetched object before the next iteration. The backend pushes/pops a LIFO stack, so this loop repeatedly reuses one object rather than holding 6000 objects. Pool fetch activates the object before returning it. | Scene `ObjectPooler.itemsToPool` in android-assets.json; `AddObject`0x1967814, especially0x1967b40–0x1967b64; `GameObjectPool.Get`0x252b66c and `Release`0x252bb28. | The local implementation follows the source loop and activation/release order. It does not reinterpret 6000 as concurrent preallocation. Whole-port validation pending. |

Its OnEnable is empty, whereas Windows starts a one-frame unparent coroutine.
