Health contacts receive a signed floored Summon-category change, without the Windows Enemy-tag restriction. | Source SpiritSummon hierarchy in remaining-character-actor-assets/android.json; `ProjectileSummon.OnEnable`0x19a0a60 and `OnCollisionEnter2D`0x19a0a64, category2 at0x19a0c14 and pool release0x19a0c68. | Source lifetime, enable/contact behavior and typed damage are implemented locally. SeekEnemy uses source player-centered pooled selection, with player fallback. Collision-matrix, pooled lifecycle and gameplay acceptance remain whole-port work; no broad claim of engine equivalence is made. |
| A103 — moth damage belongs to its child freeze pulse | The source moth's Projectile references its child FreezeOrHarm as Harmful. Assigning projectile damage therefore updates the child's damage. It has no root CircleCollider/manual trigger list and no additional root Harmful damage operation. The child freezes a nonfrozen Health target or applies its Freeze-category damage to an already frozen one. Adding an independent Bullet damage hit on the root would change that contract. | Original PF_FrostMoth hierarchy and serialized harm pointer in remaining-character-actor-assets/android.json; `Projectile.Awake`0x198e7c0 and source direct-spawn setter calls; FreezeOrHarm producer evidence under A93. | The native root and child damage values are synchronized on direct spawn, and the embedded native Bullet damage operation is omitted for this child-pulse configuration. Existing source freeze duration/category handling remains in AndroidStatusEffects.cs. Whole-port validation pending, including physical callback ordering. |
| A104 — direct projectile reuse does not reset every field | The source direct-spawn helper explicitly clears IsBurn, BurnDameage and bounce, but does not reassign piercing. Moth and fire-wave prefabs initially provide 9999 and 999999 piercing respectively. Factory-spawned shots have a separate recipe-assignment path; treating direct spawns as fresh factory recipes would introduce resets that this helper does not perform. | `ProjectileOnShoot.SpawnProjectile`0x189a74c, explicit stores0x189a990–0x189a994; original projectile fields in remaining-character-actor-assets/android.json. | Direct spawning clears the evidenced fields and retains piercing across pool reuse. No blanket fresh-shot reset is introduced. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A105 — Deer Friend loses the extra movement buff; Hail Bringer assigns one | Android Deer Friend uses AttachPrefabPowerup with one PF_DeerFriend and no extra movement-based damage action. WeaponSummon's movement/bullet/curse flag sets begin empty; this attachment does not enable the movement flag. Windows attaches an additional movement-stat SummonDamageBuff. Android Hail Bringer assigns its serialized projectiles=1, whereas Windows increments projectiles on every application. A second/repeated/Specialize application therefore differs. | Original DeerFriend and HailBringer powerup fields; `WeaponSummon..cctor`0x19a4518 initializes empty sets; `DasherSpecialProjectilePowerup.Apply`0x189ec20 assigns at0x189ec74. Native DasherSpecialProjectileAction.Activate increments. Private evidence: deer-seeker-source and original source-ledger assets. | Deer Friend now retains only its native attachment action, including one attachment per reached application. Hail Bringer assigns one projectile. No movement-damage compensation or additive projectile growth is retained. Implemented locally; whole-port validation pending. |

## Shared movement, enemy AI and persistent contacts

These source findings belong to the same uncompiled/unverified implementation batch. They are mandatory parity behavior, not a balance backlog. Private evidence: movement-ai-source/game-flows-arm64.json, movement-ai-source/physics-settings.json and movement-ai-native/. The ARM input is the same Android 8.6/1093 libil2cpp.so recorded by the source ledger.

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A106 — movement retains the physics-step boundary | Android moves every registered enabled actor by Transform position in reverse registration order, then dispatches registered IMoveUpdate callbacks in reverse order. Registration happens in LateUpdate on each enable. Disabling unregisters and zeroes the movement vector, without Windows' additional Rigidbody velocity/angular-velocity reset. No explicit SyncTransforms runs between movement and callbacks. Both original players have autoSyncTransforms=false; an eager sync would change the physics positions observed by the sampler. | MoveSystem2D.FixedUpdate 0x196d5ec and Register 0x196c7bc; MoveComponent2D.LateUpdate 0x196c7a4 and OnDisable 0x196c6a0. Original globalgamemanagers settings in physics-settings.json. | The shared movement owner replaces the projectile-only adapter. The added SyncTransforms and projectile enabled check are removed; callback registration supplies the source lifecycle. No compensating sync is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A107 — AI cadence, frozen actors and cached flocking differ | AI accumulates deltaTime and runs every third rendered frame. Frozen actors are skipped without clearing their movement vector. A cached flocking neighbor is used on the next AI update without the ordinary max-speed test, then cleared. GetClosestAI no longer rejects stationary actors by maxMoveSpeed. Flock distance is assigned 0.4 in Awake; specials require a positive range or an already-running timer. | AIController.Update 0x196ca94, especially 0x196cb08–0x196cb8c, 0x196cc3c–0x196cc68 and 0x196cecc–0x196d0c8; GetClosestAI 0x196d258; Awake 0x196c90c. AIComponent registration/disable 0x196c42c/0x196c54c. | Source cadence, registration, frozen skip, cache branches, flock radius and cooldown multiplier are implemented locally. No added velocity reset, speed clamp or fixed-step substitution. Component-only enable/disable consumers and remaining serialized/inherited reachability remain P2 coverage work; no class-wide equivalence claim. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A108 — enemy shooting specials lose their burst coroutine | Source Use emits one projectile immediately. Windows CultistShoot emits three shots with 0.5-second gaps and HasturShoot emits five. Android has no numRepeated/delayBetweenShots fields or burst coroutine here. All four shipped shooting-special assets have rotateProjectile=false. | AIShootSpecial.Use 0x18b24e0; original AIShootSpecial assets and native ShootCR. | The native shooting specials use the single-shot source path. Projectile speed, pool identity and sound match source assets. No extra burst or cooldown compensation is retained. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A109 — persistent gun-damage contacts are a list, not a set | Eligible enemy-layer contacts append GameObject IDs without deduplication; one exit removes one occurrence. Ticks walk backwards, resolve each ID through the pool registry and apply negative floor(multiplier × live gun damage). The damage loop does not filter inactive pooled targets or clear stale entries. Multiple contacts can cause repeated ticks until matching exits. Neither the list nor tick phase resets on disable. | PersistentHarmBasedOnGunDamage.OnCollisionEnter2D 0x198a51c, OnCollisionExit2D 0x198a704, Update 0x198a8ac and DealDamage 0x198a90c; ObjectPooler.AddTransformDic/GetFromTransformDic 0x1967f0c/0x1968250. | Source contact-list behavior and pooled ID resolution are implemented locally. Impact notifications translate the represented ID to the GameObject required by native observers. No deduplication, inactive-target repair or reuse reset is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A110 — Shoggoth's special cooldown doubles | Android Shoggoth serializes a four-second special cooldown; Windows serializes two. Across common-key AI assets, this is the only difference in the inspected movement/facing/special fields after retaining distinct variants per key. This bounded field comparison does not prove other-method equivalence. | Original android-assets.json/windows-assets.json, key flanne.AIComponent:Shoggoth; movement-ai-source/actor-ai-fields.json retains compared fields, variants and input hashes. | The source four-second base is applied to the native pooled prefab before allocation, so normal spawning and Endless scaling use it. Whole-port validation pending. |

## Homing, trails, arrow retrieval and salvo firing

Private source evidence: remaining-projectile-producers-source/game-flows-arm64.json, projectile-trail-source/game-flows-arm64.json, arrow-pool-overload-source/game-flows-arm64.json and the matching native exports. The following implementation remains uncompiled and unverified; acceptance validation still waits for the whole port.

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A111 — homing cadence depends on visibility and retains direction across reuse | The source tests visibility every five physics frames while visible and every thirty while hidden, using a half-unit camera-frustum bound. Hidden shots refresh their aiming direction every third physics frame; cached acceleration still applies each frame. Lost targets are retried only every tenth physics frame. OnEnable chooses a random pooled enemy and resets frame/visibility, but leaves the cached direction intact. A reused shot without a valid target can continue accelerating along its previous direction. | HomingProjectile.Awake 0x197fb6c, OnEnable 0x197fc50, FixedUpdate 0x197ff8c and OnDisable 0x19803fc. Its five original prefab instances all retain acceleration 15 on both platforms. | Source cadence, cached direction, pooled targeting, visibility and trail lifecycle are implemented locally. No immediate retarget, hidden-shot stop or direction reset is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A112 — trail enabling assigns a boolean; spawn bounce is inert | Android SpriteTrail.SetEnabled assigns a boolean and resets the spawn timer when true. Windows increments/decrements a BoolToggle counter, so repeated enable calls can accumulate. Android BounceOnSpawn retains serialized components but its compiled type has only its constructor, whereas Windows animates rise/fall and cancels a tween on disable. | SpriteTrail.SetEnabled 0x192ec9c and Start 0x192e760; BounceOnSpawn..ctor 0x1973b80 and original class method inventory. Native SpriteTrail.SetEnabled and BounceOnSpawn callbacks. | Trail enabling now represents the source boolean through the native renderer; repeated calls do not accumulate flips. The native spawn/fall/disable bounce callbacks are suppressed. No replacement animation is invented. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A113 — salvo uses pool IDs and distinguishes hiding from release | Android selects unlocked targets from monster pools using strict center distance, without the native 250-collider limit or Passive-tag exclusion. Locked IDs resolve again during firing. Indicator updates and OnDisable use raw SetActive(false), while firing explicitly releases indicators to their pools. The former paths therefore leave pool-list/counter membership intact. Source OnDisable clears locked IDs and its coroutine reference; Windows only clears indicators. Missing ID resolution during indicator updates skips the entry rather than repairing the lists. | SalvoShooter.GetClosestUnlockedEnemy 0x197bd78, UpdateIndicators 0x197bb88, OnDisable 0x197c2b4 and SalvoShootCR.MoveNext 0x197c770; ObjectPooler.GetMonsterInRange/GetInRadius 0x1968a9c/0x1968b60. | ID-based targeting, distinct deactivation/release paths and source teardown are implemented locally while retaining the native base shooting/input path. Salvo is removed from the blanket native deactivation-to-release rewrite. No stale-entry repair or extra active-target filter is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A114 — retrieved arrows activate at their source position and return through their own pool | Android fetches each return projectile with its world position supplied before activation, under that pool's parent. Its return coroutine follows the current player and reads retrieveSpeed on each step, then releases the projectile without Windows' explicit reparent-to-ObjectPooler/local-position reset. Retrieval-point Start/OnDestroy are one-instruction tail calls to register/remove, not empty methods; their existing native registration remains applicable. | ArrowRetrieveOnReload.OnReload 0x1982f24 and RetrieveCR.MoveNext 0x1983438; ArrowRetrievalPoint.Start/OnDestroy 0x1982d3c/0x1982e6c; pool-position overload 0x252b0f8. Direct-call evidence: arrow-electro-direct-callers.json. | Position-before-activation, source pool-parent overload, live retrieval speed and final pool release are implemented locally. Native point registration is retained. No extra explicit position/parent reset is added. Whole-port validation pending. |

ElectroProjectile reachability note: the original Android inventory has zero serialized instances of this class. Its dump contains its four own methods, with no ElectroProjectile metadata-method, type-metadata or string creation reference; the all-executable aligned direct B/BL scan finds no constructor caller. Its OnTrigger would generate thunder before invoking Projectile.OnTrigger, but this compiled class does not establish an obtainable weapon or active gameplay producer. It is recorded as dormant at the inspected original-asset/direct-reference boundary; no speculative weapon or effect is added. This does not assert that arbitrary dynamic reflection is impossible.

## Shared contact damage, spores and line geometry

Private evidence: shared-contact-source/game-flows-arm64.json, original harmful hierarchies and layer-names.json, line-templates.json, shared-contact-native/, and electro-player-source/game-flows-arm64.json. These are source findings and local implementation work, without current-batch compilation or runtime acceptance.

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A115 — contact damage is typed and has no Unity trigger-entry/onHarm path | Android Harmful targets its enemy layers, rejects a player Health, applies the stored damage category and mutates its own damage when the reduction affix halves it. Source PostNotification sends impacts from the player only for OnPlayer damage; other categories send from the Harmful component. HarmfulOnContact inherits this collision path and has no separate Unity OnTriggerEnter2D, onHarm event or native HitNotification. Constructors explicitly default to OnPlayer. Magic Bow return damage is MagicRetrieving; spore explosions are Powerup, not Bullet. | Harmful.OnCollisionEnter2D 0x197d754, OnTrigger 0x197d7d4 and constructors 0x197dc58/0x197dc68; original 48 source Harmful/HarmfulOnContact records and 16 native contact counterparts. | The native contact boundary now follows source categories, sender selection and collision-only behavior. Old character/explosion-specific Harm prefixes are replaced by the shared owner. Native onHarm/HitNotification are not invoked. Prefab layers/body/contact settings follow the original matching roles; the Boomer explosion gains its source kinematic body and stops using a trigger, Deer hitboxes use Bullet collision role, and Fireball retains its source 0.2 radius. Dynamic producers keep their live damage and proc flag. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A116 — knockback no longer bypasses immunity or excludes by Passive tag | Source Knockback accepts only enemy layers, requires a MoveComponent2D and respects knockbackImmune. It has no Windows ignoreKBImmune override or separate Passive-tag exclusion. Direction still uses the producer's previous movement vector when available, otherwise the position difference. | Knockback.OnTrigger 0x198057c and physical forwarding callback 0x1980550; native Knockback.OnCollisionEnter2D. | Physical and sampled projectile contacts share the recovered source implementation. No immunity bypass, tag-based exception or additional magnitude clamp is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A117 — Spore Flower targets pooled centers and advances through projectile dispatch | Source Attack samples the complete strict-radius monster-pool candidate list instead of Windows' two-collider buffer. Spray uses up to sprayAmount entries from that list rather than the native amountSporeTrack-sized collider buffer; amountSporeTrack remains serialized but is not read by the inspected source spray method. OnProjectile advances the counter; there is no ordinary physical collision callback. Explosion damage still uses the same two-stage ceiling calculation and optional Bullet Damage scaling, but its actual damage category remains Powerup. | SporeFlower.Attack 0x19a195c, OnProjectile 0x19a1cb4, SpraySpores 0x19a1d38 and OnSporeHit 0x19a2074. Original fields match the existing native flower's numeric recipe. | Native357/358 verifies actual Spore Gun attachment/base-pulse omission, source automatic targeting/strict radius-five boundary and real arc/Powerup11 explosions. Temporary source stat/conditional scaling inputs give two-stage ceiling45/58. A real gun projectile crosses seeded counter4→0, triggering seven arcs: six actual tracked pooled targets despite unused tracking5, then one random-radius arc. All six tracked arcs physically damage targets. Active arc/counter3/clock2.3 are omitted on exact source Continue; ordinary restart clears the flower and old launches. Source code remains unchanged; no new tracking cap/bug correction. Explicit geometry/stat/counter fixtures do not prove natural frequency or evolution/legacy/Custom/delivered boundaries; whole-port acceptance remains open. |
| A118 — line collider width, allocation and stale segments change | Source uses atan2 plus sin/cos with the full LineRenderer startWidth as its offset, versus Windows' slope division and half-width. It creates separate polygon/body objects per segment, feeds world-space quad coordinates at world origin and retains cached segments when the point count shrinks or loop turns off. The source template is a separate inactive Collider with an electron tag; the native template is the entire live ElectroLine renderer/controller root. Source LineController resets its four targets to serialized local corners on enable and refreshes positions from those targets. | LineCollider.SetPolygonCollider2DPath 0x1980f08 and CalculateColliderPoints 0x1981204; LineController.OnEnable 0x19814e8/GetPositions 0x1980df4; original line templates. ELF PLT resolves 0x382ef70/0x382efb0/0x382efd0 as sinf/cosf/atan2f. | Source geometry/corner/cached-segment code is implemented locally without cloning the native controller recursively or pruning stale segments. The source electron identity is represented by an owned marker because Windows lacks that registered tag. A119–A122 now wire its player, movement, blocking and despawn consumers locally. This is uncompiled/unverified implementation, not completed line gameplay parity; remaining coverage and whole-port acceptance still block P2. |

## Player contacts, arena boundaries and shared protection

Private evidence: electro-player-source/game-flows-arm64.json, player-collision-followthrough-source/game-flows-arm64.json, player-protection-movement-source/game-flows-arm64.json, player-feedback-source/game-flows-arm64.json, original player-hierarchy-android.json/player-hierarchy-windows.json and wall-identity.json under electro-player-source. Source ARM input remains SHA256 e780cc48f4f01f83b8322e9587311152280bf313f8cd9e9241c2dc9f21aee559. The following are static findings and local uncompiled/unverified implementation, not device observations.

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A119 — player collision order and invulnerability timing differ | Android consumes a dodge roll before asking Health to apply damage, even while invulnerable. A successful dodge only displays its localized popup; the Windows dodge minipause/radial knockback/sound callbacks are absent. Contact damage is applied in single-heart steps. Health invokes onHurt only for remaining red-HP loss, so an entirely absorbed soul-heart hit does not invoke that event. Ordinary contact starts 0.2-second knockback at seven units/sec. Hit invulnerability flashes/waits in 0.4-second steps until its requested duration is reached: a request of one second yields three waits, nominally 1.2 scaled seconds plus frame scheduling. The source OnHitInvicibility flag is an ordinary bool, not an overlap counter. | PlayerCollider.OnCollisionEnter2D 0x18583dc; KnockbackCR.MoveNext 0x18594f4; InvicibilityCR.MoveNext 0x1859388; Health.HPChange 0x1979574 and Hurt 0x197e060. ELF constants 0xa15b54=0.4 and 0xa15b84=0.2. Original source onHurt retains Hurt_SFX only, not Windows' minipause/radial effects. | Source contact order, per-heart changes, events, knockback, flash cadence and invulnerability are wired to native PlayerHealth in AndroidPlayerCollision.cs. Windows-only persistent hurt/dodge FX are omitted from these source paths. No one-second clamp, source-loop timing correction or overlap-counter repair is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A120 — player hitbox and arena movement correction change | Source PlayerHitbox uses a kinematic full-contact body, an enabled radius-0.35 circle and a disabled box; Windows uses a dynamic body and enabled box. Rectify casts along current movement direction for the proposed step length plus 0.35. If the first hit is an electric edge or wall, it translates by the hit-point distance minus 0.35, without clamping negative displacement. Knockback's boundary test uses one radius-0.35 overlap at the current hitbox center, not the destination. All 24 original Android Wall-layer objects have tag Wall; their 24 name-matched native counterparts lack that tag. The source DespawnRange object is inactive, although its callback body otherwise matches Windows. | PlayerController.Rectify 0x198bf38, MovePlayer 0x198b524, Translate 0x198c2fc; PlayerCollider.GetElectronDir 0x1859038. Original player hierarchies, TagManager and complete Wall-layer inventory; DespawnRange.OnCollisionExit2D 0x187d5dc. | Circle/body/box settings, source raycast/displacement and overlap semantics are written locally. Wall identity is adapted through the verified native layer; electric identity uses its owned marker. Native keyboard/controller input and aiming remain. DespawnRange is disabled to match the source object. No extra collision sweep, destination test or movement-distance clamp is added. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A121 — Holy Shield shares the same invulnerability counter as hits and skills | Source PreventDamage.ApplyInvincibility flips Health.isInvincible; after its 0.5-second break it unflips that same counter. Health.HPChange invokes onDamagePrevented for a negative change rejected by any invulnerability contribution. Shield, hit and skill interactions therefore cannot be represented by an independent isProtected boolean/counter alone. A119's subsequent collision branches also read that shared invulnerability state. | PreventDamage.ApplyInvincibility 0x196201c, InvincibilityCooldownCR.MoveNext 0x1962314; Health.HPChange 0x19795ec–0x1979620. These source fields supersede the earlier adapter comment claiming an Android isProtected field. | AndroidProtection now records shield contributions for continuation while adding/removing those contributions in native PlayerHealth.isInvincible. Restoring the shield count adjusts its contribution rather than replacing other owners' flips. The obsolete native CheckCollision protection-write transpiler is removed because source contacts now own that boundary. Earlier A79 shield fixtures did not prove this newly corrected shared-counter interaction. Whole-port validation pending. |
| A122 — electric edges survive player hits but can be released by Wind Shield | Player enter/stay special-cases electron: it does not release the edge, and Stay starts knockback/invulnerability without applying another HP change. Ordinary enemy projectiles are released after player contact; PF_Laser contacts disable only their collider. Wind Shield's ProjectileShieldRune checks only the enemy-projectile layer, with no electron exception. Its release can therefore deactivate a line segment on contact. PoolMap.Release falls back to SetActive(false) for an unregistered name, and LineCollider's cached-segment branch updates paths without reactivating that object. This is the source consequence if that shield/edge contact occurs, not an Android runtime reproduction. | PlayerCollider.OnCollisionEnter2D 0x1858a18–0x1858cd8 and OnCollisionStay2D 0x1858e08; ProjectileShieldRune.OnCollisionEnter2D 0x1854004; ObjectPooler.Release 0x1965ebc; GameObjectPoolMap.Release 0x252b230; LineCollider.SetPolygonCollider2DPath 0x1980f08, activation only in its allocation branch at 0x198104c. | Player/laser disposal and electron stay handling are wired locally. Existing Wind Shield layer filtering/release remains source-equivalent; unpooled release uses the source deactivation branch. Cached line segments are not automatically reactivated. No shield exclusion or arena-edge repair is introduced. Whole-port validation pending. |

A119 / user M10 follow-through — soul-heart damage and no-damage unlocks: the red-HP-only onHurt event must remain Android behavior. It cannot also be used as the sole signal for the user's separate no-damage achievement condition, because a hit absorbed by soul hearts would then leave that condition intact. The local implementation records accepted soul-heart/red-HP damage in an independent run flag before damage callbacks can trigger a boss death, and persists that flag for Continue. Damage prevented by invulnerability does not clear it; healing does not restore it. This preserves the explicit user unlock requirement without changing Android combat-event dispatch. Old checkpoints fall back to their saved native hitless flag, whose earlier Windows event path included soul-heart damage. Current implementation is uncompiled/unverified; soul-heart loss, prevention, callback ordering and continuation still require whole-port acceptance.

## Actor physics and enable/disable coverage

| ID | Finding and gameplay implication | Source evidence | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| A124 — some enemy actors have no Rigidbody2D | Of 100 original decoded AIComponent records, 76 omit a root Rigidbody2D and none of those inherit one from any Transform ancestor. These are repeated scene records for twelve names: BrainMonster, EyeMonster, Lamprey, PF_SpawnedBug, PF_TreeMonster and its (1), (4), (8), (9), (12), (14), (15) variants. Their matching Windows actors have extra kinematic bodies. A kinematic body and no body are different contact configurations; this does not establish a dynamic pushing force or a played balance symptom. | Private movement-ai-source/actor-body-ancestor-ownership.json contains every original root and complete ancestor chain; electro-player-source/actor-body-comparison.json retains the platform comparison. Native full IL confines MoveComponent2D.Rb reads to movement/disable paths already replaced by the port. | AndroidAIRecipes.ConfigureTemplate removes the extra body from an inactive owned template before spawning. AndroidPools.SourceTemplate supplies those templates to pool allocation and native, Endless, Challenge and Continue map creation. Original prefab assets are retained. This is source-driven implementation, not an arbitrary collision repair. It remains uncompiled/unverified; actual contacts, spawning, reuse and map continuation are whole-port acceptance requirements. |

Enable/disable reachability remains a bounded coverage finding, rather than a new alleged gameplay bug. The compiled Windows Behaviour.set_enabled callers inspected do not toggle AIComponent or MoveComponent2D; the source direct-call scan likewise identifies no such toggle. The original serialized scan covers 593 AnimationClips: all 228 script-property bindings target UnityEngine.UI.Image. It finds no MonoBehaviour payload containing set_enabled and no decoded persistent call to that setter. Private evidence: movement-ai-source/behaviour-enable-direct-callers.json and component-enable-serialized-bindings.json. This narrows the component-only AI lifecycle question but does not prove computed/reflection entry points impossible. Native MoveComponent2D has a disable callback covered by the port; the AI companion covers GameObject disable. No speculative global setter hook or invented caller is added. Observable contact coverage and remaining source reconciliation stay open in P2; no class-wide physics-equivalence claim is made.

## Timed difficulty modifier

- A125 — the late-run +1 enemy-damage modifier has no established update producer. Both original builds configure D10_EnemyDamageModifierAfterTime with additionalDamage=1 and timeRemainingToActivate=300, referenced at index 10 of DefaultDifficultyLevels. Windows starts a coroutine that waits one frame and then timeLimit minus 300 scaled seconds before adding damage. Android ModifyHordeSpawner at 0x196c114 only stores the spawner. Its non-virtual Update at 0x196c11c contains a one-shot strict timer > timeLimit - 300 test and addition, but the class derives from ScriptableObject. That method is not an automatic player-loop callback for this owner. All file-backed executable aligned B/BL references, original method/type/string metadata, all 8,326 decompressed MonoBehaviour/ScriptableObject payloads and all AnimationClip events identify no caller or serialized Update binding. This supports an inert modifier at the inspected shipped boundary, not an Android runtime reproduction or proof that arbitrary reflection is impossible.

  Private evidence: timed-enemy-damage-source/game-flows-arm64.json, update-callers.json and update-serialized-bindings.json; original platform asset inventories and native EnemyDamageModifierAfterTime decompilation. Engine contract: Unity's [2023 ScriptableObjects guide, page 9](https://assets2.brandfolder.io/bf-boulder-prod/3fctx42nqsmx4f9jj7gmjn3q/v/1131033164/original/create-modular-game-architecture-in-unity-with-scriptableobjects.pdf#page=10) and [2022.3 ScriptableObject messages](https://docs.unity3d.com/2022.3/Documentation/ScriptReference/ScriptableObject.html) distinguish supported asset lifecycle messages from MonoBehaviour Update.

  AndroidDifficulty.cs suppresses the Windows coroutine entry. No component or timer is introduced to run the uncalled source method, and no balance correction is made. This local implementation remains uncompiled/unverified. Legacy checkpoints can already contain the earlier Windows damage addition in their raw spawner modifiers; converting that saved contribution remains an explicit P2-G3 acceptance blocker, together with the broader recipe/schema conversion. The existing saved data is not blindly decremented.

## NPC health, Forest encounter and pooled drop consumers

- A126 — direct NPC feedback and cleanup are different from the retained event fields. Source Health.HPChange (0x1979574) applies signed category/global modifiers, updates health and feedback, then posts its typed surviving-hurt event or calls Death. Negative changes to dead targets stop early; nonnegative changes are still processed, including healing from zero without a subsequent death/hurt notification when the previous HP was zero. Hurt (0x197e060) calls the first child PlaySoundEffect, root FlashSprite and damage popup directly, in that order. It does not invoke the NPC onHurt event. Death (0x197dea4) posts Health.DeathEvent with the damage enum while the actor is still active, then releases it, plays the first child sound, spawns each DeathSpawnObj in serialized order and optionally shakes. It does not invoke NPC onDeath. This affects reentrant tree activation, kill upgrades, pool membership and feedback/drop order.

  The serialized onDeath event is not deleted from the source contract: set_maxHP (0x197b394) still invokes it when the maximum becomes nonpositive. The exported event recipe can differ from ordinary Death. For example, BigBoomer's event contains a prefab-spawn callback and Shake, while its ordinary Death uses the string drop list and DeathShake=false. The 32 distinct shipped non-player Health recipes are generated with their initial maximum, ordered drops, shake flag and explicit event callbacks; all repeated source records for a name must agree. Forest tree initial HP is 90,000 rather than Windows' 45,000. Existing source wave/boss spawners continue to assign their own spawn-time health afterward.

  Source OnKillTrigger.OnEquip/OnUnEquip (0x185e330/0x185e478) still subscribe either to the general death name or the legacy Health.{type}DamageKill string. The reviewed Death producer emits only the general typed notification. The port does not synthesize the legacy category notification or rewrite that legacy listener to a new producer. The separately recovered specialized summon-kill powerups now consume the general typed event and filter Summon, retaining their actual source contract.

  Local implementation: AndroidHealthEvents.cs, generated AndroidHealthTables.cs and tools/export_android_health.py; source-aware ChangeHealth/TakeDamage/HPChange/AutoKill wiring; specialized kill consumers and arena ownership use scoped typed death notifications. The actor mapping is to original source recipes, not a blanket claim about arbitrary added Health components. Original NPC shield/fragile producer coverage, remaining source consumers, legacy recipe conversion and whole-port acceptance remain P2 work. This is uncompiled/unverified code, with no game or build run.

- A127 — the apparent extra Forest tree is a renamed/repositioned source role. Windows has PF_TreeMonster (17) under all eight Forest/Endless tile instances; Android has two distinct PF_TreeMonster objects per tile instead. Complete original hierarchies show nine tree roles on each side, with unchanged tile/root transforms and matching inspected box-collider fields. Several other suffixes also correspond to different positions, so matching on suffix alone is wrong. The explicit original-role pairing maps all nine native trees to source local position/rotation/scale and names before the owned inactive template loses its extra rigidbodies. Pairing is resolved during generation; the runtime has no nearest-tree heuristic. Initial health comes from A126's source recipes. No tree is removed to make the inventories appear equal.

  Source ForestMapRhogogSpawner.Start (0x1981b24) registers Rhogog/walking-tree pools and scoped Health death observers. OnTreeKilled (0x1981d8c) sets activation once and replaces each active tree with a pooled walking tree at its position. Because the source notification precedes release, the dead tree's active state is part of this ordering too. OnOneSecondLeft (0x1981ed0) checks treesActivated without Windows' additional Endless exclusion, stops the timer, fetches Rhogog from its pool and places it 20 units to the player's right. This handler does not create an arena. The requested M10 secret-boss unlock/notice handler remains the explicit user override for the reward path. AndroidForestEncounter.cs, generated AndroidForestTables.cs and tools/export_android_forest.py implement this through the existing owned-template and pool owners. Actual activation, encounter completion, continuation and restart remain whole-port acceptance, not established gameplay results.

  Supporting source consumer work: SpawnFromObjectPool.Spawn (0x1999490) explicitly skips a null pooled effect, allowing later drops to proceed; the native helper's dereference is replaced by that source branch. SpawnPrefabFromObjectPool.Spawn (0x19999a8) instead retains its initialized flag and unguarded fetch. Its four matching source attachment owners supply their serialized 10/100 values as visibility limits (A128). SpawnHarmfulObjOnNotification.Start/Spawn (0x199977c/0x1999624) likewise supplies visibility=100 and reads the current player gun, with position/damage assignment after pool activation. FreezeOrHarm's reviewed Health-only filter, signed Freeze category and serialized three-second duration already match the local source path; no second replacement is added.

- A128 — the parameter's meaning changed while the caller field name did not. The original source signature is AddObject(string tag, GameObject go, int visCount=-1, int max=99999, int initCount=0). SpawnPrefabFromObjectPool and SpawnHarmfulObjOnNotification load amountToInitInObjPool into ARM64 w3 (visCount), set w4 to 99999 and w5 to zero. BigBoomer/Luna's corresponding prefab effects therefore have visibility 10, Headshot/Curse effects visibility 100, and Implosion's notification effect visibility 100. These values do not request 10/100 allocations or change the active-object cap. A89's hiding/retained-renderer behavior applies when a visibility limit is reached. Registering an already-existing tag returns without replacing its pool configuration, matching AddObject's ContainsKey branch. The local registrations now map the source parameter explicitly. No artificial warm-up is retained. Evidence: original dump signature, dynamic-pool-registration-source and forest-contact-followthrough-source Start bodies, plus global-pool-source AddObject (0x1967814, existing-tag exit at 0x19678e4 and true initCount loop at 0x1967b24). This corrects an integration-time argument interpretation; it does not fix Android's gameplay.

Private evidence: forest-contact-followthrough-source/game-flows-arm64.json, the three windows-flanne.*.cs exports, forest-tree-layouts.json, tree17-ownership.json, tree17-source-identity.json and authenticated managed-identity-evidence.json; forest-clock-death-source/game-flows-arm64.json and windows-Health.cs; shared-health-event-order-source/game-flows-arm64.json; typed-death-trigger-source/game-flows-arm64.json; death-drop-pool-consumers-source/game-flows-arm64.json. The native managed identity is SHA256 586ee2209220361f0b141f383b9aee430fc16bd450820394828c835d3d185547, MVID 541e796c-ca56-4a1b-8ba9-ed8634531cc2. Static parsing/decompilation did not execute the game or validate the new code. The temporary REA export was copied with matching digest, authenticated on E: and cleaned with transaction e438891786a5bf8f149a2cc60080eb1b after the native workflow exited; the durable evidence remains.

## Glass and notification-owner follow-through

- A129 — Glass's source state belongs to one perk asset. TomeOfGlassPowerup.Apply (0x18a2e58) first applies its stat recipe, registers its OnTweakDamge delegate, sets the player's Health.fragile flag, then adds Damage=2 to its private _damege field. OnTweakDamge (0x18a3000) adds one order-zero MultValueModifier using that current field. With one retained asset and repeated applications, the factor is 2, 4, 6, rather than independent native two-times modifiers producing 2, 4, 8. OnDestroy (0x18a2f44) removes the observer and zeros _damege. The class does not override ClearData or the runtime-save hooks. Asset unloading and same-process versus fresh-process replay are therefore part of acceptance; no per-run reset or new persistence is inferred.

  Same-process lifetime follow-through, classified before the affected acceptance rebuild: original generator Start/ResetAllPowerupRuntimeData (0x1986ce0/0x1987018) copies original asset references and invokes virtual ClearData. Inherited Powerup.ClearData (0x198d270) writes only level1 at offset0x50. FindPowerupByName (0x19887d4) returns an existing list member without cloning; RestorePowerups (0x186416c) calls that same ClearData and replays ApplyAndNotify. Glass has no override resetting its accumulator at0x64. Consequently a retained original asset at6 becomes8/10/12 when three saved acquisitions replay. Windows runs348/349 rejected an incorrect fixture expectation of6; read-only owner traces in349 establish the same instance and increments, with no destruction. No production reset/unload is added. G/Q/U records a source-confirmed conditional lifetime rule with unknown natural Android retention/intent, not an independently established Android bug. Evidence: private glass-observer-lifetime-source/warm-pool, warm-reset and warm-find exports; failed fixture output is retained.

  Source Health.HPChange (0x1979574, fragile branch 0x1979624-0x197964c) checks fragile after invulnerability rejection but before shield absorption, damage modifiers or hurt dispatch. Health.Death's player branch (0x197df18-0x197df28) directly sets HP to zero without emitting onHealthChange/onHurt or consuming soul hearts. Windows' original Glass instead kills through OnPlayerHurtTrigger. The local native adapter writes the HP backing field and invokes native death listeners to enter Windows' existing death flow; its independent M10 no-damage flag is cleared before that callback. The source multiplier is represented by a Unity asset referenced from the original native devil-profile recipe. Copied Windows application recipes resolve that canonical owner; there is no static owning cache, DontDestroyOnLoad or added run reset. Implementation: AndroidDamageTomes.cs and AndroidPlayerCollision.cs. This remains uncompiled/unverified, including actual asset release, protected hits, soul hearts, repeat acquisitions, continuation and native restart. The G/Q classification does not assert that the source repeat rule is a deliberate refinement or a confirmed bug.

- A130 — Android's notification center rejects an already-registered equal delegate. AddObserver (0x1929cdc) obtains the sender list, calls Contains at 0x1929f94 and returns on a match before invoking-list copy or append. Windows' matching method appends unconditionally. This matters when the same source Glass asset re-registers its callback. The local shared prefix follows the source equality rule; it does not collapse distinct helper instances or different sender scopes.

  Android also calls NotificationCenter.Clear (0x192a624) from the title-to-battle coroutine at 0x186efe8, retry at 0x187c394 and return-to-title at 0x187c7b0. Clear empties existing callback lists and sender dictionaries, then the root table; it does not clear the separate invoking-list set. Windows has no Clear method and its corresponding three coroutines proceed directly to loading. AndroidNotifications.cs inserts the source clear at each native load instruction, preserving native wait/unpause and A84's earlier live shooting-state exit. The owned Save & return path clears at its corresponding load boundary too. These are scoped source lifecycle semantics, not a global SceneManager interception, cleanup retry or invented safety layer. Native PostNotification/RemoveObserver/Clean have not received a whole-class equivalence claim. All affected repeat, transition, observer lifetime and Continue acceptance remains pending.

- A131 — SoulConversionPowerup's compiled name does not identify the shipped Soul Conversion/Soul Expand recipe. The complete original 8,326-MonoBehaviour/ScriptableObject inventory has zero SoulConversionPowerup or ShieldHpTransformationHp instances, one TomeOfGlassPowerup and two SoulShieldPowerup instances, with no inventory errors. Original sharedassets1 asset 3597 is named SoulConversion but its script is SoulShieldPowerup, localization key soul_expand_name, shieldHp=1, shieldHpMax=2, amountToAttach=0 and prefab=null. SoulShieldPowerup.Apply (0x18a1898) adds capacity and directly assigns the player's increased shield value. This agrees with the existing local Soul Expand direct-grant recipe, not a timed conversion feature.

  The separate compiled SoulConversionPowerup.Apply (0x18a0fc8) publishes Event_TransformationCDUpdate. HealthUI listens and starts its static cooldown at -1; Refresh dynamically adds a conversion helper only when that value is positive. The helper then converts its represented soul heart after a strict deadline, once. The reviewed aligned B/BL scan identifies only HealthUI.Refresh as an Init caller and no direct SoulConversionPowerup.Apply call, but does not exclude virtual/indirect/reflective creation or every computed event publication. Classification is N/U at this boundary. No selectable timed perk, positive cooldown or recurring timer is added to manufacture reachability. Further demonstrated source producers remain required review work.

Private source evidence: health-conversion-glass-source/game-flows-arm64.json, direct-references.json, owned-recipe-and-reachability.json and shield-grant/game-flows-arm64.json; soul-heart-ui-owner-source/game-flows-arm64.json; glass-observer-lifetime-source/game-flows-arm64.json; observer-transition-boundary-source/game-flows-arm64.json. HPChange/Death evidence remains under pool-health-popup-source and forest-clock-death-source. Native observer, copy and transition IL comes from offer-counts-specialize-source/windows-powerup.il, corresponding to native assembly SHA256 586ee2209220361f0b141f383b9aee430fc16bd450820394828c835d3d185547. Original APKM and ARM hashes were rechecked unchanged. These are static implementation findings, not an Android device reproduction or validation of the current port. A129-A131 are classified above; the user's review before build/game validation remains open while implementation continues.

## Wind/Might repeat ownership and player healing

- A132 — Wind and Might do not acquire another damage callback on each repeat. TomeOfWindPowerup.Apply (0x18a3538) registers the original asset's OnTweakDamge delegate without accumulating a damage field or applying a stat recipe. The source observer equality check in A130 leaves one callback. OnTweakDamge (0x18a36a0) reads the live MoveSpeed.Modify(1), adds MultValueModifier(order 0, 1-DamageLost) with shipped DamageLost=0.15, then adds MultValueModifier(order 1, movement) only if that factor is positive. A zero movement factor therefore leaves the 0.85 modifier instead of zeroing damage. There is no native speed floor substituted for this source branch.

  TomeOfMightPowerup.Apply (0x18a31e4) applies its +1 MaxHP stat on every application and then registers its same asset callback. That callback supplies its unchanged Damage=2 once, with the signed consequence in A73; it does not add another damage modifier per repeat. The earlier local per-pick DamageBuff recipe was therefore not source-equivalent for repeats. AndroidDamageTomes.cs now resolves one canonical original devil-profile asset owner for Glass, Wind and Might; it replaces those three native recipes without changing their native stat application. Glass alone accumulates the A129 field. Native positive-damage versus source signed-damage conversion remains explicit for Might. Original-asset lifetime, interleaved equal-order modifiers, repeated acquisitions and same/fresh-process Continue still require whole-port acceptance. G/Q describes these rules without asserting beneficial tuning or fixing A73.

- A133 — player healing and contact damage share source HPChange semantics. HPPickup.UsePickup (0x18572ac), KillEffectPowerup.OnKill (0x18a03a0) and HealOnBurn.OnInflictBurn (0x19869bc) reach HPChange with Cure; Health.Heal (0x197e180) itself is an empty return. Windows' corresponding actual producers call PlayerHealth.Heal. The local native Heal boundary now enters the signed player-health adapter (Cure represented by its native None category), rather than treating the empty Android API as the healing implementation.

  Negative input observes dead/invulnerable/fragile checks, then soul-heart absorption and residual red hurt. Player changes bypass the NPC damage-modifier stages. Zero input still assigns/clamps HP and invokes the health-change event, but does not emit onHeal. Positive input emits onHeal for the requested positive amount even if clamping leaves HP unchanged. An original zero HP permits healing/event delivery but returns before subsequent surviving-hurt/death dispatch. For an originally living target, that final typed notification is also emitted for surviving positive/zero changes; its name alone does not imply actual damage. Contact damage still calls the adapter in the source single-heart steps. M10 damage tracking remains separate and is not cleared by healing. Current implementation and all its event/continuation/native-input consequences are uncompiled/unverified; no new death recovery behavior or arbitrary healing-producer equivalence is claimed.

- A134 — source player-health event helpers use a live global owner. OnPlayerHPAboveHalf/BelowHalf and OnPlayerHealedEvent/OnPlayerHurtEvent resolve PlayerController.Instance.health. Native helpers cache GetComponentInParent<PlayerController>().playerHealth. The local adapter replaces that lookup and the relevant cached reads, including threshold initial-check coroutine reads and destructor unsubscribe. It retains the existing native UnityEvent bridge; these source callbacks discard their int value. The half-health bodies use float HP/maxHP and their exact orEqualTo branch, matching native comparisons; their initial coroutine yields one frame on both platforms. Null event invocation branches also match. These bounded method matches do not prove all attachment acquisition paths or whole-class/whole-engine parity. No callback is added solely because a class exists.

- A135 — Angelic changes its actual healing producer. Original Android Angelic asset 3481 attaches one PF_HealOnSmiteKill on each application. Its original root 1235 contains only Transform 1899 and HealOnSmiteKill 4167, with killsToHeal=500 and recover=1. Start (0x195ffa0) subscribes to SmiteKillNotification and OnDestroy (0x1960054) removes that observer. OnSmiteKill (0x195ff48) increments its own counter, resets at threshold and calls player HPChange(recover,Holy). The native selected perk instead used the category-specific OnKillTrigger; a compiled native HealOnSmiteKill class existed but was not that perk's attachment. The source Smite producer publishes its nominal kill notification for surviving/protected captured targets too (A79), so Angelic must count those notifications rather than silently restoring a true-kill filter.

  AndroidRemainingPerks.cs now attaches the native-compatible helper per source application and supplies the original 500 threshold. Its callback uses live player health, a one-heart grant and the source Holy category through native Smite. ResumeRuntime includes each helper's partial counter. Soothing Warmth retains its already-reviewed 0.0005 burn-roll action, now reaching the shared signed healing adapter; it is not replaced by a new deterministic cadence. Old inline-Angelic checkpoints require semantic conversion under G3. Native Start/observer cleanup is reused at the actual player-owned attachment boundary, not as a blanket class-equivalence claim. Source nonkill counting is classified B via A79 and preserved; acquisition/repeats, protected/nonlethal Smite, healing, save/Continue and restart are unverified.

Private evidence: wind-tome-health-events-source/game-flows-arm64.json and source-recipes-and-native-boundaries.json; health-threshold-coroutines-source/game-flows-arm64.json; player-heal-producers-source/game-flows-arm64.json; other-player-heal-producers-source/game-flows-arm64.json; angelic-heal-owner-source/game-flows-arm64.json and original-prefab-components.json. Source Might Apply/OnTweakDamge remains in evidence/S3/extended-gameplay-arm64.json, with original serialized recipes in the source inventory; observer equality is A130. Native comparisons/callers use the retained SHA-bound full IL. All changes belong to P2's uncompiled/unverified batch. The pre-build ledger review stays open, and classification does not authorize source bug corrections.

## Shared summon initialization and pulse configuration

- A136 — summon stat ownership and detachment change. Source get_summonAtkSpdMod (0x199fcc0) and get_summonDamageMod (0x19a0040) read the current PlayerController's stats on every call. Source Start (0x19a2fb4) does not cache the native player/stats fields; dontParent uses SetParent(null,false) before virtual Init. OrbitalWeapon/SeekerWeapon Init call base Init, subscribe via the live getter and assign their initial modified speed/acceleration. Their destructors unconditionally remove their handlers through that same live getter, including objects which never ran Start. There is no source equivalent of the old local UninitializedSeekerCleanup guard. This is not proof that any global-player destruction order is harmless.

  SummonLifecycle.cs now replaces the shared getters, removes native cache writes, translates the parenting overload and supplies source base initialization at its actual native counterparts. Retained native Lens/Spore reads of the protected player field are replaced with live lookup; ProjectileSummon's source Init only performs its base call and an unused GetComponent<SeekEnemy>, without the native cached-player assignment. ShootingSummon retains native cursor input and source pool registration, while its native producer-hit observer is omitted. SetHitboxActiveSummon inherits base Init on Android, so the native extra producer-hit subscription is omitted there too; source surviving-health consumers remain the event contract. A14's old guarded Windows teardown evidence is historical and does not validate these new lifecycle changes. G/Q classifies the established source rules; teardown, detached poses and retained native consumers remain unverified.

- A137 — not every summon reaches the pulse initializer. Source base Summon.Init (0x19a0714) adds a DurationDamageBuff, assigns Summon damage classification, configures the first such component through UpdateDurationDamageData (0x19a2ff8), then subscribes to Summon_DurationDamage_Update. OrbitalWeapon, ProjectileSummon, SeekerWeapon, ShootingSummon and StabbingSummon explicitly call it. BulletEnhanceSummon and SetGameObjectActiveSummon inherit it, as does SetHitboxActiveSummon. SporeFlower.Init (0x19a1858) and ThunderAttackSummon.Init (0x19a3854) do not call base; the complete inspected bodies only initialize their own pool/generator fields. Consequently those two paths do not gain a pulse/listener through Start. This is a source quirk rather than an assumed mandate to repair their inheritance. Original PF_SporeFlower and PF_ElectroBug component/prefab identities are evidenced; ElectroBug's existence alone is not proof of an obtainable feature.

  The original ElectroBug powerup3540 is a member of DefaultPowerupProfile and attaches PF_ElectroBug after Electro Mage. It is obtainable, not inferred from the prefab alone, closing that initial U facet. PF_SporeGun's AttachPrefabToPlayerOnStart3836 references PF_SporeFlower. The earlier local blanket Summon.Start postfix and scene-wide pulse scan are removed. Initialization now follows the listed source paths, including repeated base initialization's add-component/first-component distinction. Source pulse damage is a flat floor(damage*1), not another SummonDamage stat transform: the configured values are damage50, interval2 and radius3. Update uses a reverse pooled-ID query and resolves each ID when applying damage, with no new active-target test or timer reset on reuse. Pulsing Summons' separate +20% SummonDamage stat still applies normally. Q records these source rules without presuming a defect or claiming whole-class equivalence.

  Shared A81 cadence covers the native ElectroBug and SeismicWard owners, completing the six native AttackingSummon subclasses at this base-method boundary. SetGameObjectActiveSummon.Attack (0x19a0f34) matches the native activation/true return body. ElectroBug's two attacks retain source damage22/9x5 range and last-target success; selection follows ObjectPooler.GetRandomEnmey (0x19686d8), which ignores the supplied summon center and queries around the current player. ElectroBug and Tentacle use that source pooled-center query. The earlier ElectroBug recipe-only equivalence note is superseded for this dependency. Native351/352 now verifies actual repeated acquisition, omitted base pulse, shared generator, successful/failed Update interval subtraction, strict player-centered targets with actors parked25/30 units away, two physical34-damage contacts after Mastery, live-stat33+12 contacts45, exact current Continue with unsaved partial clocks omitted, and ordinary restart. The temporary stat operand is removed before save; fixture orbit/prerequisite parking, controlled targets and stopped scheduling do not prove natural encounters/frequency. Other pulse/actor/legacy/Custom and delivered boundaries remain open; no production behavior or Android bug is corrected.

- A138 — pulse configuration is listener-owned. PulsingSummonsPowerup.Apply (0x18a26ec) applies its stat change, sets static DurationDamage=true and posts the three values joined with literal commas using current-culture Single.ToString. It does **not** assign static DurationDamageData. Each registered Summon.UpdateDurationDamageData casts/stores the payload, sets the first helper's enabled state, then parses damage with Int32.Parse and interval/range with Single.Parse. Base Init initially calls that same handler with the current static data. If Apply runs with no registered listener and data is empty, a later helper is enabled with zero/default damage, interval and range. No direct data assignment or invented listener is added to fix that condition.

  This is a conditional source defect, not a reproduced natural Android run. Trainer's original prerequisites include Magic Spear, Scythe, Ghost Friend or Dragon Egg. Original Dragon GameObject1226 is enabled; SummonEgg.Start (0x19a3284) disables it before scheduling its hatch. The egg alone therefore does not prove that Summon.Start failed to run: script Start order and actual acquisition/lifecycle conditions still need evidence. Summon.Clear (0x19a31ac) resets the static bool/string. Its inspected direct callers are the retry and return-to-title coroutines, after notification Clear and before scene load; the title-to-battle path has notification Clear without Summon.Clear.

  The adapter now preserves that event/data order and those reset boundaries, with the owned Save & return path resetting after capture. New checkpoints record the static enable/data state and each persistent pulse's damage/interval/range/clock, so rebuilding helpers does not by itself normalize an unconfigured owner. Old Timer-only pulse providers and blanket Spore/other helper records still require G3 semantic conversion; existing strict checkpoint validation is not removed. All current edits are uncompiled/unverified and belong to ACTIVE P2. No build, player run, acceptance validation, commit or release occurred.

Private evidence: summon-global-lifecycle-source/game-flows-arm64.json, direct-base-init-and-clear-references.json, original-prefab-enable-states.json and source-contract-and-native-boundaries.json; summon-derived-init-source/game-flows-arm64.json, egg-start/game-flows-arm64.json and attack-consumers/game-flows-arm64.json. PulsingSummonsPowerup.Apply and DurationDamageBuff.Update/get_FinalDamage remain in evidence/S3/powerup-classes-arm64.json and powerup-backends-arm64.json. Native source is the retained hash-bound full IL; transition ordering is in observer-transition-boundary-source/game-flows-arm64.json. Direct branch coverage does not prove absence of arbitrary indirect/reflective callers. A136-A138 are classified before the required final pre-build review; no independent bug fix is authorized.

## Dragon Egg deadline and continuation

- A139 — Dragon Egg's hatch timing and save state change. Source Start (0x19a3284) hides the summon and assigns hatchSuccessTime=GameTimer.gameTime+secondsToHatch, marks initialization, then applies any pending restored remaining time. It no longer starts the native full-duration WaitForSeconds coroutine there. Update (0x19a3474) starts hatching only when gameTime is strictly greater than the deadline. Before starting the animation it sets isHatchSuccess=true. The source coroutine (MoveNext0x19a36d8) then flashes three times at 0.2-second waits, plays the native particles/optional sound, activates the summon and disables the egg. The configured duration remains180 seconds; this is not a new hatch-speed buff.

  GetRemainingHatchSeconds (0x19a35ac) returns -1 once that success flag is set, including the period when the egg is still visible and the dragon is still inactive during flashes. Otherwise it returns max(0,deadline-gameTime). RestoreRemainingHatchSeconds (0x19a3638) queues a nullable value before Start and applies it immediately afterward. ApplyRestoreRemainingHatchSeconds (0x19a3360) treats only negative input as complete: it activates the summon and hides the egg without hatch effects. Nonnegative input hides the summon, activates the egg and sets a new relative deadline; zero still requires a later strictly greater game clock. The source restore method does not stop an existing coroutine or add another reset on enable. These are source rules/quirks, not a confirmed Android bug or permission to normalize the flash phase.

  AndroidDragonEgg.cs now owns the source fields/lifecycle missing from the Windows type and uses the existing native summon, flash helper, particles and sound. Its visual coroutine runs on the native egg owner so native disable/destroy semantics remain. The sibling Update observes the native component's enabled state; its execution begins only when native Start initializes the source state. The old EggStarts/Time.time registry, pre-Start save rejection, rewritten secondsToHatch and restarted full-duration coroutine are removed. Persistent capture uses source success/remaining semantics, mapped to the existing hatched/remaining fields: old hatched records still activate immediately, while unhatched records supply their recorded remaining duration. Old zero-remaining records cannot distinguish the original pre-deadline/flash frame; no more precise legacy phase claim is made. Actual acquisition, pause/deadline boundary, pre-Start restore, hatch-phase save, continuation and restart remain unverified.

  FlashSprite's six source methods were inspected as a presentation dependency: they manage a cooldown and renderer/material state, with no gameplay stat/health mutation. The egg's source/native flash recipes both use0.1 seconds and the red flash material; native uses one repeat. The native presentation helper is retained under M1, without claiming its new CDData implementation or arbitrary overlapping visual calls are equivalent. Source/raw native method and original selected prefab fields substantiate this limited reuse, not a new gameplay port or rendered proof.

- A140 — relative deadlines need the restored clock. The previous Windows RunResume.Restore ordering invoked ResumeRuntime.Restore before assigning the saved GameTimer value. An egg with90 seconds remaining restored against a new-scene clock of0 would acquire deadline90, then a jump to saved time100 would already exceed it. This is an integration ordering error, not an Android defect. The saved clock/warning flag are now assigned after loadout/content resolution and before acquisition replay, following the source timer-before-powerups order established in A141-A146. This supersedes the earlier placement immediately before final runtime restoration. Other snapshot owners and raw saved fields remain unchanged by this ordering change; their integrated consequences still need final validation. No timer offset, delayed repair, duplicate restore or timeout is added.

Private evidence: dragon-egg-clock-source/game-flows-arm64.json (13 bounded methods), flash-presentation/game-flows-arm64.json (six methods), source-contract-and-native-boundaries.json, and the retained native full IL for SummonEgg Start/WaitToHatchCR and FlashSprite. Original selected recipes come from the hash-bound Android/Windows inventories. These are static implementation findings. A139 is G/Q and A140 is I; neither adds a likely-Android-bug family. P2 remains the sole ACTIVE stage, and the whole-port pre-build review, remaining source coverage, legacy recipe/provider/stat conversion and final integrated delivery remain acceptance blockers. No build, game run, acceptance validation, commit or release occurred.

## Ammo, reload and Generator event consumers

- A141 — source ammo changes have different producers. Ammo.Start (0x1977530) creates its infinite-ammo toggle, then init.MoveNext (0x197799c) waits for the source WaitForEndOfFrame. It calls Reload, emits OnAmmoChanged again, emits OnMaxAmmoChanged and subscribes to MaxAmmo changes. This is a source frame boundary, not an added readiness wait. Reload still fills maximum ammo, emits amount change, then OnReload. UseAmmo (0x1977768) skips only for infiniteAmmo, otherwise subtracts/clamps/emits amount; it has no Windows ShouldConsumeAmmoCheck veto. GainAmmo (0x1977824) clamps/emits amount without the separate native OnAmmoGained notification. RestoreAmmo (0x1977914) also clamps/emits amount. The inspected exact-zero/full equality getters and max-stat callback otherwise match the native boundary.

  AndroidAmmo.cs implements those changed paths and source startup ordering. The native Windows-only veto/gain-event paths no longer drive gameplay; Siege already uses its actual source RefundAmmoWhenStanding gun-shot listener/refund. Its original source Start/Update/callback/teardown were compared to that native helper. The decoded native Ammo instance has no persistent OnAmmoGained or OnReload calls; its persistent amount/max handlers update UI text. This bounds the serialized receiver check in addition to the native IL consumer census; it does not cover arbitrary dynamic binding. The older RefundAmmoWhileStillBuff veto implementation is not its reconstructed recipe. OnPlayerReloadEvent now subscribes/unsubscribes through the current player, retaining its matching null-conditional callback. Native continuation uses the source clamped amount-change boundary; A143 now places the reconstructed Generator first-Start listener after that notification. A91's startup callback is now evidenced at end-of-frame; the actual relative creation/Start timing of its reload-spawn receiver still remains within that finding's stated limit.

- A142 — Generator is not restricted to ammo gains. Original PF_FireRateBuffOnAmmoGain contains FireRateBuffOnAmmoGain3934 and BuffPlayerStats3643, with duration2 and a single FireRate+0.35 change. Its Start (0x1971be0) registers ApplyBuff(int) with OnAmmoChanged, not a gain-only event and not a frame-difference sampler. Every notification, including shots, reload, duplicate startup amount delivery and full/clamped changes, reaches the same callback. ApplyBuff (0x1971d44) removes an already applied bonus, sets its active flag/time and applies the bonus again. Update removes it only when gameTime>applyTime+duration. It has no destructor unsubscribe/removal.

  The historical native FireRateBuffOnGainAmmo repair in A36 is superseded for the current source recipe. Generator now creates the actual pair of helpers per reached application, using AndroidGenerator for the missing source event/deadline state and native-compatible BuffPlayerStats for the original stat recipe. The earlier local flag/time provider is superseded by A143's source-count replay; source does not serialize this timer. Whole-port runtime/stat reconciliation and actual continuation remain required G3 acceptance work. Earlier gain/refresh fixtures do not verify this changed event path.

- A143 — the saved buff counter can outgrow the applied bonus. BuffPlayerStats.ApplyBuff (0x1971dd0) increments _stacks before processing its array. RemoveBuff (0x19721a8) reverses one recipe without decrementing that counter. AddStack is a tail call to ApplyBuff; RemoveStacks removes the recipe _stacks times and then clears the counter. Thus Generator's remove/reapply refresh keeps one active35% contribution but increases _stacks on every notification. AttachPrefabPowerup.TryGetInGameSaveRuntimeValues reads the paired helper's count; RestoreInGameSaveRuntimeValues reaches RestoreInGameSaveStacks (0x1973c04). It first compares max(0,requested) to the existing count and returns if equal; otherwise it removes a positive old count, clears it and replays positive requested applications. The save writer emits this runtime value only for a positive count. Those records omit the original root ordinal; restoration consumes successive matching helpers, compacting any zero-count holes. This establishes the source counter/replay mismatch, not a measured post-Continue total or proof of every initialization interleaving.

  Source application/AddStack counter behavior and live global player/stat reads are now adapted on the native helper. Native Start's parent-cache assignment is suppressed, matching the empty source Start. The selected positive FireRate branch agrees; other special HP/pickup/vision side effects remain bounded by their separate consumer coverage, not a whole-class equivalence claim. The source restores powerups/runtime values synchronously inside InitState.RestoreFromSaveCR, after its scene initialization boundaries. Newly attached Generator helpers have not received Start at the later RestoreWeapon ammo notification. Its active flag/time are absent from AttachPrefabPowerup runtime save values; continuation does not preserve that timer. The local Windows path now saves the paired source count separately from its actual live stat contribution, removes only that known contribution from the raw FireRate snapshot baseline and invokes the source count consumer. It no longer restores Generator active/time fields. During native acquisition replay this specific listener stays disabled until ammo restoration ends, then Unity delivers its first real Start; no notification is replayed to it. This bridges the native yielding restorer to the evidenced pre-Start source boundary, rather than adding a gameplay safety guard. Schema5 distinguishes this source-count checkpoint. Released v3/v4 native gain-only Generator providers/active BuffCR records and their saved attachment-action identity are converted in memory: the known active contribution seeds one application, while an inactive helper seeds zero. Lost historical Android event counts are not invented and the owned checkpoint file is not rewritten by reading it. Other source runtime/stat owners and legacy recipes still require G3 reconciliation. Current and migrated Generator contribution/count/start behavior, rounding and repeated same/fresh-process Continue remain unverified; this is not whole-checkpoint parity.

- A144 — source Ammo's removal method is called OnDestory (0x1977640). Its body removes the MaxAmmo handler, but its spelling is not Unity's automatic OnDestroy message. The aligned B/BL audit finds no direct caller at this boundary. The native automatic OnDestroy unsubscribe is therefore suppressed instead of repairing the source spelling. This is Q/U: no cross-run live-stat leak or Android runtime error is claimed, and arbitrary computed/manual invocation is not ruled out by the scan.

- A145 — reload polling and lifecycle change. Source ReloadState.Enter (0x185b42c) creates and starts a new reload coroutine, starts ReadFireCR and only at the end assigns IsForce from outOfAmmo. The held-fire loop checks once per frame for Fire==1 and nonempty ammo; it changes to ShootingState and then stops the live reload field. OnFireAction additionally checks the retained force flag. Completion captures reload duration once, fills ammo/plays the end sound and uses Fire!=0 to choose Shooting versus Idle on the native input branch. Exit unsubscribes, resets animation, hides the bar and stops the read routine; it does not independently stop or null the reload routine. Fire/disable cancellation performs the source state-change-before-stop order. Source Idle.Enter has no native queued empty-ammo coroutine; its ammo-change handler remains the automatic reload producer.

  AndroidReload.cs implements those source state fields/routines through the native state machine and the existing Windows Fire/Reload bindings. Mobile joystick callbacks and their auto-shoot field are platform input, not a new Windows mobile-control dependency. Native gun audio override remains presentation. Native Idle's extra queued entry reload and pause checks inside the two changed callbacks are removed at these source boundaries; integrated pause/input behavior must still be validated with the native UI/action maps. No timeout, null-stop guard, duplicate ammo-change forwarder or coroutine-field normalization is added. Core pause/menu and other shooting-state paths still require their own coverage. Acquisition/reload, gain during reload, held/released/analog fire, state re-entry, exits, source quirks and fresh-process Continue remain unverified.

- A146 — ManualReloadState's definition does not establish another reached mode. The supplied metadata has its private callbacks/iterator but no StateMachine.ChangeState<ManualReloadState> instantiation or standalone type-info entry in the reviewed script map, and the complete original component inventory reports zero instances. Source Idle.OnReloadAction explicitly targets ReloadState. No new state/setting is created from the compiled class alone. This N/U disposition does not prove every reflective or string-created path absent; a demonstrated producer would require renewed implementation review.

The source restorer's actual top-level call sequence (0x186405c) is RestoreTimer, RestorePowerups, rune runtime, weapon, spawners, XP/score and drops. A140's clock assignment is consequently moved earlier again: after native loadout/content resolution and before acquisition replay, not merely before final runtime fields. RestoreWeapon reaches Ammo.RestoreAmmo after restoring powerups. This refines the earlier clock boundary from direct source evidence; it does not close the remaining G3 runtime/legacy conversion.
