## Dragon growth recipe and continuation follow-through

- A147 — the old Windows perk path never attached the patched growth helper. Original Windows Aged Dragon758 and Trained Dragon761 use PeriodicTrigger60 with limitActivations=true/limit20 and ModShootingSummonDamageAction15 or ModShootingSummonAttackSpeed0.1. Original Android AttachPrefabPowerup3515/3518 attach one PF_BuffDragonDamageOverTime or PF_BuffDragonAttackSpeedOverTime on each application. Their helper4306/4676 fields are Dragon,60 seconds and15 damage or0.1 speed. The original Windows inventory contains no serialized instances of those helper classes; the previous mod prefix changed a compiled helper Update without changing these actual perk recipes. A38's formula description was correct, but that local patch did not implement the reachable recipe. This is an integration gap alongside an Android gameplay change, not an Android bug.

  AndroidCombatAttachment now creates the source helpers, including full repeated applications, and replaces the native periodic actions. The old helper-only speed patch is removed. Source Update uses strict timer>60 and advances one tick per frame, subtracting60 before adding deltaTime; it has no twenty-activation cap. Damage adds15; speed divides cooldown by1.1 only while the current value is greater than the original0.33 float. It does not clamp the result back to0.33. Counters advance even when no live cached target changes. Actual acquisition/repeats, post-twentieth growth and native restart remain unverified.

- A148 — growth owns a cached target list and source save state. Both source Start methods read the current player, collect matching ShootingSummon children including inactive objects once, set the initialization flag and reset the applied count. They do not add summons acquired later to that cache. RestoreRuntimeState invokes Start if not initialized, computes max(0,savedCount)-oldCount and applies only a positive difference. Damage uses one delta-times15 addition per cached survivor; speed repeats the threshold/division for each missing tick. It then records the nonnegative count even when that is lower than the old count, without reversing existing growth. Its timer becomes max(0,60-max(0,remaining)). These rules are G/Q: the lower-count branch is not evidence of a naturally reached bug. The initialization check is an actual source field, not a new Windows readiness guard.

  The native helpers lack the initialization flag and applied counter; per-helper weakly held state now supplies those source fields while the native components retain their list, recipe and Update lifetime. Schema5 captures count and remaining time. Current reconstruction initializes these source attachments explicitly, uses original actor damage/cooldown rather than copying already-grown snapshot values, and then replays the source count. The source-unsaved per-actor attackSpeedMod is likewise reconstructed on this speed-growth path. This prevents duplicate growth and prevents the old additive Windows Trained Dragon bonus from remaining alongside the new cooldown rule. The helper's later Unity Start is idempotent, matching source. No new observer, timeout, elapsed-run estimate or growth reversal is added.

  Released v3/v4 checkpoint conversion reads each actual periodic PerkEffect activation count and observed remaining delay, keeps its acquisition identity, and changes that effect owner to the new instant attachment recipe in memory. Native PerkEffect.OnTriggered increments only before accepted limited actions and stops at20, so those counts describe executed Windows applications. Counters are not estimated from run duration. Reading does not rewrite the owned file. Other changed recipes and source runtime/stat owners still require P2-G3 reconciliation; this selected conversion does not establish whole-checkpoint parity. Current/legacy continuation, inactive eggs, cache membership, multiple helpers, threshold crossing, lower-count restore and same/fresh-process workflows remain final whole-port acceptance work.

Source: BuffSummonDamageOverTime Start0x1898b9c/Update0x1898d3c/Restore0x1898f38; BuffSummonAttackSpeedOverTime Start0x18985b4/Update0x1898754/Restore0x1898968; AttachPrefabPowerup runtime save/restore under A143. The damage-restore tail at0x382ebc0 resolves its missing metadata load and branches back to0x1899008; it is not a second gameplay operation. Private evidence: stat-growth-save-source/game-flows-arm64.json, restore-tail-and-speed-constant.json and source-contract-and-native-boundaries.json; original recipe/profile inventories and the retained native IL. A147 G/I and A148 G/Q are classified before any build/game validation. A143 additionally has an I facet: provider-only Generator conversion left its saved native attachment-action identity incompatible with the reconstructed recipe; that identity now converts too. The source counter bug remains unchanged. All current code remains uncompiled/unverified/uncommitted. P2 is the sole ACTIVE stage; G1/G2/G3, final pre-build reconciliation and whole-port delivery remain open.

## Ritual, In the Wind and Justice runtime owners

- A149 — Ritual's reached recipe and saved data change. Windows Ritual735 uses an unlimited OnKillTrigger with Curse classification,10 kills and ModStatAction Bullet Damage+0.01. Android AttachPrefabPowerup3478 attaches PF_DamageBoostOnCurseKill/helper4279, which listens to CurseSystem.CurseKillNotification. The previous mod had not replaced the native trigger/action recipe. OnCurseKill0x196b0e8 increments the counter, grants+0.01 at10, clears the counter and adds to its accumulated float. The current source curse producer supplies the named notification (A94); no legacy typed-kill publisher is invented. Start/OnDestroy register/remove that actual listener and the stat lookup uses the current player.

  Source saving records the accumulated damage even when zero, and omits partial kill progress. Restore0x196b2cc resets kills, assigns the saved float and adds that float to Bullet Damage when nonzero; it does not remove an old contribution first. The local recipe now creates the actual helper per application, and its missing accumulated field is tracked per native component. Current checkpoints store that field and reset partial kills. Older native unlimited effects leave _activations at zero, so their total historical Ritual applications cannot be recovered from that field. Conversion changes the saved effect owner, starts new source accrual at zero and preserves the known existing total in the aggregate stat snapshot; it does not fabricate curse-kill history or rewrite the owned file during read.

  P2-G3 remains an internal acceptance blocker here: current numeric stat totals still use the existing aggregate checkpoint, while source helper data is translated separately. Exact source additive-call order, fresh-baseline reconstruction and observer consequences, including interactions with movement/dodge providers, remain to implement/reconcile. No duplicate stat addition, synthetic notification or observer-suppression patch is added to disguise that gap. The aggregate legacy contribution must remain preserved when that path is reconciled. This is G/I/U, not completed source save parity or an Android bug correction.

- A150 — In the Wind owns accumulated damage and movement. Source helper4162 and native helper936 both specify0.1 damage,0.1 movement,10 seconds and maxTicks4. Source Update0x1985624 adds to the accumulated floats after each actual stat grant. OnHurt0x1985720 removes those floats and clears ticks, timer and both accumulators; Windows instead multiplies its tick count by the recipe during removal. Source Start/OnDestroy resolve the current player's hurt event. The ignored source integer hurt argument is bridged to the existing Windows parameterless event; red-only hurt delivery remains A119, not a new event publisher.

  Restore0x19857cc clamps ticks into0..maxTicks and timer to at least zero, but restores the accumulated amounts independently without a matching clamp. It adds those amounts to the corresponding stats and restores the sprite-trail threshold, finding the trail when needed. The live update stops adding time at maxTicks but still processes an already-due timer, so no extra max-tick guard or catch-up loop is substituted. The local live path now tracks/removes exact accumulations and restores their data/trail through schema5. Released native counter/timer snapshots translate their known tick grants into the new fields. Current aggregate stats supply the saved numeric contributions once; the broader source mutation/observer replay gap stated under A149 remains open. No large-timer or Android-device symptom is claimed from the retained branch alone.

- A151 — Justice does not preserve a partial500-event counter. Source helper4607 saves its earned maximum-health count, including zero. Restore0x195ff04 clears the partial kill counter and clamps earned count into0..mhpGainCap. It does not grant/heal health or add a MaxHP stat again; the source top-level health restorer handles the saved actual health. The local helper now uses live current-player lookups for its retained cap/assignment arithmetic and source notification lifetime. New snapshots store earned count rather than partial kills. Older helper records or capped native Justice effect activation counts translate that earned count, discard partial progress and preserve saved actual health/stat totals. Smite's nonkill notifications still count (A79); no kill-only correction is introduced.

All three original Android roots contain only Transform plus the named helper and no children; their recipes/lifecycle are reconstructed without missing attached artwork or extra effects. Helpers created during continuation receive their first subscription Start after reconstruction, matching the source synchronous restore boundary. Current/legacy numeric state, subsequent hurt/cure/curse/Smite delivery, repeated helpers, partial-progress reset, trail state and same/fresh-process continuation remain unverified. The source mutation/observer replay and other legacy owner conversions remain P2-G3 work; G1/G2 coverage and final whole-port delivery remain open. ## Asset-owned kill effects and rune continuation

- A152 — Feed the Beasts, Bloodsuckers and Soul Drain share KillEffectPowerup, with thresholds15/500/500 and Summon damage filtering. Apply0x18a0284 first applies the ordinary stat recipe, registers the original asset's death handler and independently parses its effect string as float and integer using the current-culture overloads. Each successful parse adds to the retained per-grant amount. Repeated applications therefore share the counter and accumulate strength; source notification deduplication prevents duplicate copies of that asset's callback. Feed grants its float to Summon Damage; Soul Drain adds its integer to soul HP; Bloodsuckers calls signed HPChange with Cure0 (native None); A210 corrects the earlier default-Bullet source interpretation. OnDestroy0x18a0398 clears only the integer/float grant amounts. ClearData0x198d270 resets only PowerupLevel to1; it does not clear these counters or the accumulated damage. The local recipes now resolve an owner through the original native default profile instead of retaining per-copy native kill actions. These asset-lifetime and parsing rules are G/Q; no independent run-reset correction is authorized or supplied.

- A153 — TryGetInGameSaveRuntimeValues0x18a051c emits only the Summon Damage accumulator when it is not approximately zero, as summon_damage_acc with round-trip/invariant formatting. Neither partial kills nor accumulated per-grant float/integer amounts are saved, and the two heart-grant perks emit no runtime value. Restore0x18a0724 assigns a parsed accumulator then adds it to Summon Damage when nonzero, without removing a previous contribution. Current schema6 captures that source field and retains the source partial-counter omission. The existing aggregate-stat restoration still supplies its numerical total: the source additive call and per-powerup observer ordering remain the same internal P2-G3 blocker identified in A149. Older native unlimited Feed effects did not increment their activation count, so conversion preserves their known aggregate bonus without fabricating the missing grant history. Saved legacy effect identities convert in memory to the new application action; reads do not rewrite the owned file. This is G/I/U, not completed source Continue parity.

- A154 — SoulAttunedRune Init0x1854dd0 increases its limit by rank, clears the granted count, adds that capacity and calls InvokeRepeating(DropHP,timeToDrop,timeToDrop). The shipped timeToDrop is300 seconds. DropHP0x1854e68 adds one soul heart while below the limit. Source saving records only the granted count; Restore0x1854fc0 clamps that count to at least zero, with no upper-limit clamp or timer restoration. Healing's Restore0x185090c similarly restores its nonnegative kill counter. The local Body and Soul helper now starts InvokeRepeating directly from rune initialization instead of decrementing a preserved remaining-delay field. Schema6 omits that field; older provider records discard it in memory. The source reset of timer phase on fresh reconstruction is G/Q, not an independently corrected bug.

## Ordered source powerup restoration

- A155 — Diamond's Berserk and Bulk Up use original PF_BuffOnHurt/PF_BuffOnHeal roots with BuffPlayerStats plus OnPlayerHurtEvent/OnPlayerHealedEvent. Their persistent calls invoke ApplyBuff, with Fire Rate/Reload Rate+0.1 or Bullet Damage/Projectile Size+0.15. Native Windows instead has unlimited OnPlayerHurtTrigger/OnPlayerHealTrigger plus ModStatAction, so earlier live stat equivalence did not supply the source count-bearing runtime owner. Both local recipes now attach the source helper/event pair per application. Pyromaniac's original BuffOnBurn root has OnBurnEvent→AddStack and OnPlayerReloadEvent→RemoveStacks, with Fire Rate+0.1; Generator is the fourth BuffPlayerStats root. The source attachment save dispatcher emits only positive counts and its reader consumes successive matching helpers without retaining zero-count root holes. New source capture/restoration now covers all four keys separately. Old unlimited native Diamond effects did not record their total grant history; their effect identities translate in memory while known aggregate bonuses remain preserved, without fabricated counts. A155 is G/I/U; actual current/legacy event and count behavior remains unverified.

- A156 — Android RestorePowerups0x186416c processes unique taken powerups in first-taken order. It clears that powerup's level data, applies its saved level-minus-one acquisitions, updates the pool/taken owners, and invokes that powerup's runtime handler immediately. The top-level restorer handles actual health, rune runtime, weapon/ammo and the other saved owners afterward. It does not restore an aggregate StatsHolder snapshot. Only the eight AttachPrefabPowerup helper families and KillEffectPowerup's damage accumulator have powerup runtime handlers at the reviewed source boundary; only Healing and Body and Soul override the rune save handler. Unsaved temporary effect phases, shield cooldowns and generic native provider fields are not added to this source runtime contract.

  Current source consequence, S4/run-242/244/245: the interleaved original Glare build has60/30 damage before Might,58+28=86 after it. Android first-taken grouped continuation creates both Glare helpers before applying the three Intense Glare upgrades, so both reconstruct at60 and deal58+58=116. Actual native readback confirms two15x4 helpers, two typed damage/impact callbacks and unchanged outside/unpooled targets. Both source-unsaved helper clocks start at0. The old fixture expected86 and failed243/244;245 passes the source116 contract and restart. This is a demonstrated consequence of A156, not a new balance correction or postponed implementation.

  Fresh runs now capture schema7 source powerup/rune values and replay acquisitions in their first-taken groups. Egg, both Dragon growth types, Ritual, In the Wind, Justice, Specialize, all four counted-buff roots and Feed's accumulator restore immediately within their owning group. Ritual/Feed/nonzero In the Wind amounts invoke their source additive stat APIs; no aggregate overwrite or AddFlatBonus(0) notification substitutes for those calls. Zero In the Wind amounts skip the corresponding stat call, as its source branches do. Native evolution replay and ammo restoration follow health/rune restoration. Count-bearing attachments and their event components receive first Start afterward; the native shield's actual initialization boundary remains available for later shield upgrades. No arbitrary wait or observer-suppression mechanism was introduced.

  Specialize GetInGameSaveLvUp0x1989d28 returns1 when unused and0 when used. Restore0x1989d34 sets used for a nonpositive saved value and clears its static count only on that path. Its later Start still increments the count; the port now preserves that ordering instead of restoring after Start. The source eligibility quirk A97 is retained. Legacy Windows provider lists omit destroyed spent helpers; their reconstructed missing roots now restore as used instead of being destroyed again. Saved surviving providers retain compact ordinal order, while all reconstructed helpers receive the source restore-before-Start boundary. This closes the selected lifetime mapping in source, without claiming recovery of destroyed-root identity or an old Specialize fixture. Native Windows input, the user's saved-position behavior, progression rules, Custom settings and ownership boundaries remain requirements.

  Released and earlier experimental aggregate checkpoints still use the legacy reconstruction path and continue writing schema6 while that run remains active. This prevents a subsequent save from silently losing known bonuses whose unlimited old effects never recorded their history. It does not close legacy source-semantic parity: reconciliation of that path is an internal P2-G3 advancement blocker, not a scope exclusion or tracked deferral. Fresh/current/legacy same-process and fresh-process continuation, acquisition permutations, source Start order, Custom controls, native interactions and the remaining G1/G2 consumers still need the final whole-port checks. No build/game validation or final parity claim accompanies these source edits.

## Source restore health/XP callbacks

- A157 — Three old native aggregate-replay protections were still affecting the fresh source path: TemporaryStatBuff.OnAttach was skipped during all acquisition replay; health-threshold callbacks were skipped for all restoration; Growth.OnLevelChanged was likewise suppressed. The source restorer does ordinary powerup application and emits actual health/XP callbacks. Those old guards now apply only to aggregate-format legacy reconstruction, whose known stat totals still need preservation/reconciliation. Fresh schema7 does not make replayed temporary buffs inert or skip those health/Growth callbacks. This is correction of a port integration mistake, not an Android bug fix or a new observer-suppression path.

  RestoreHealthAfterPowerups0x1865030 sets maximum HP, calls RestoreHP, assigns soul capacity and calls the soul-HP setter, in that order. RestoreHP0x197e1cc clamps red HP and emits its health event without using hurt/heal feedback. The source maximum-HP setter0x197b394 emits maximum-HP change before assigning/emitting red HP: its increase branch directly adds the difference without another clamp; its non-increase branch clamps against the live maximum after observers run. The local maximum setter now follows those writes/branches through the native backing field and event. The former nested native hp setter introduced an extra increase-branch clamp. Fresh restoration now follows the source red/soul order; native hp's clamp-and-health-event setter supplies RestoreHP's matching boundary.

  PlayerXP.RestoreState0x1859908 assigns both saved fields, then emits level, next-level XP threshold and current XP, in that order. Fresh schema7 now follows that order rather than the earlier threshold/XP/level order. HPThresholdStatRune.OnHpChange0x1852cf4 has no restoration guard; its real threshold state can therefore follow the reconstruction's health events. LastStandRune/SecondWindRune compiled methods also lack such a guard, but complete original mono inventories contain zero instances and no RuneData points to either. They are not evidence of a reached Android revival exploit or permission to add selectable content. Native compatibility of existing code remains separately bounded.

- A158 — Growth is actually bound: original GrowthRuneData3151 is visible/open, points to GrowthRune, and its original helper uses levelPerHPDrop=5 and xpMultiplierPerLevel=0.04. Init0x1851bd4 adds its rank XP bonus and subscribes OnLevelChanged0x1851b30 to the player's level event. The handler tests only level modulo5, then starts WaitToDropHP; it does not require an actual level increase. RestoreState's unconditional level event therefore reaches that handler on a qualifying restored level. The source coroutine subsequently fetches the original heart pickup from the pool at the rune's position. No source rune-runtime override persists a last-awarded level for Growth; only Healing and Body and Soul override that protocol. Repeated continuation can consequently schedule the reward again without another earned level. This is a likely conditional Android bug (B/Q/U), retained for parity. No actual Android-device drop, visual state or collection was reproduced, and no duplicate-award guard is added.

Legacy aggregate semantics, other source owners and save eligibility remain internal G3 work; G1/G2 and the final anomaly review remain open. P2 is the sole ACTIVE stage. ## Shield pre-Start state and the fresh restore boundary

A72 follow-through, 2026-10-23: the old Windows PreventDamage.Start creates cooldownRate, clears its coroutine, caches parent health, subscribes and applies protection. Android has no cooldownRate field. Its constructor0x19622f8 initializes the current/last hit counts to1 and the next-apply deadline to-1; its original Holy Shield recipe has cooldownTime120, reduceTime0 and PreventDamageCount1. ReduceCD0x196229c first advances an existing positive deadline, then adds to reduceTime. It does not replace cooldownTime or apply protection. The source real-cooldown getter0x19622dc is max(10,cooldownTime−reduceTime), so pre-Start Stalwart does not require a native rate object.

The local cooldown entry points now create only the source field owner when needed, retaining the initial deadline/counts and applying reductions to their source field. The old no-owner arithmetic that changed the native base cooldown has been removed. The final-cooldown getter supplies the source formula directly instead of executing a native getter which dereferences the uninitialized rate first. Bind no longer caches a player/protection object; protection and Start/OnDestroy event ownership resolve the current player as source does.

Source Start0x1961f68 clears the coroutine, subscribes to current-player damage prevention and calls ApplyInvincibility. Source OnDestroy0x1962088 removes that current-player listener without a new cleanup guard or protection reversal. The local Start retains that sequence and native patched ApplyInvincibility entry point, preserving source reduction/deadline changes made beforehand. A native rate object is initialized solely for existing legacy storage/consumers; source arithmetic ignores it. No protection is applied merely by reading or reducing cooldown before Start.

Fresh schema7 no longer yields between powerup groups waiting for the native rate. This removes an opportunity for reconstructed summons/event helpers to receive Start before later source groups/runtime calls. The initial native scene boundary remains; older aggregate-format reconstruction retains its existing initialization boundary while its semantic migration remains open. This is an A72 port integration correction, not an Android bug, independent balance change, synthetic callback or timeout workaround. No new anomaly ID or likely-bug mechanism is added. Current pre-Start reductions, first subscription/protection, repeated shields, source order, native visuals/inputs and current/legacy continuation remain uncompiled/unverified whole-port acceptance work. G1/G2/G3 and the final anomaly review remain open; P2 is the sole ACTIVE stage.

Private evidence: shield-prestart-restore-source/game-flows-arm64.json contains9 source method ranges; source-contract.json binds constructor/lifecycle/cooldown bodies, original source/native shield recipes and events, native Start/field IL and changed implementation hashes. Source shared invulnerability/hit behavior remains bounded by A79/A121. No build or game validation ran.

## Legacy Boolean shield migration

A159 — the published v1.0.0 writer uses snapshot version3, with shield ordinal, active flag, elapsed timer, remaining break flash, base cooldown and raw rate modifiers. It lacks source count/contribution/deadline fields. Repeated Holy Shield originally stores HolyShieldCDRAction; Stalwart also stores that native action. New source recipes attach independent shields on repeats and use AndroidStalwartAction, so strict old effect identity restoration failed before reaching shield data. Those known action identities now translate in memory without inventing activations or changing the owned file during Read.

The native final-cooldown getter calls StatMod.ModifyInverse: (base+flat)/((1+bonus)×reduction). The migration uses that original expression to recover a recorded inactive shield's remaining delay, then anchors it to the restored game clock. An already-due recovery has zero remaining delay; no arbitrary extra wait is supplied. Active and recorded flashing states map to one source contribution; inactive states map to none. The flash's recorded remaining time is retained and subsequent recovery uses the source cooldown. First-root Stalwart reduction follows the acquired applications. New source shield copies retain their actual initialized state instead of copying the original first shield's hit to every root. Missing old protection-count data derives from those owner states, preserving separate non-shield invulnerability contributions through the existing counted restoration boundary.

An earlier Boolean adapter is distinguishable by its stored AndroidStalwartAction identities: it subtracted60 from the native base per application and used a ten-second minimum. For that known layout, the old remaining delay uses the old minimum and the recorded base subtraction is moved into source reduction, avoiding a second subtraction. The transient read-time count of these old actions is not serialized. Retained version3 fixtures include both active records and a recovering base-zero record with two such actions (S4/run-66); no captured old flashing record was located. The flash translation is supported by the published writer and native coroutine, not by a player reproduction in this batch.

Already counted source snapshots retain their recorded counter when present. Unrecognized mixed Boolean/counted-owner records remain outside the known migration contract and are preserved on failure; this narrows the old blanket rejection rather than adding a gameplay guard. This selected migration does not resolve unknown permanent-bonus attribution, every changed old effect/provider recipe, or full legacy source replay. These remain actionable internal P2-G3 blockers. Current/legacy repeated shields, phase timing, cooldown revisions, shared invulnerability, resaving and native/Custom integration remain uncompiled/unverified. A159 is a port-format integration issue, not an Android bug or a new balance correction.

Private evidence: legacy-shield-migration-source/source-contract.json binds the v1.0.0 writer/tag, original native shield/rate/action/coroutine IL, earlier adapter source, retained fixture fields/hashes and current implementation. Original source constructor/cooldown/lifetime evidence remains under shield-prestart-restore-source. P2 stays the sole ACTIVE stage; G1/G2/G3 and the final anomaly review remain open. No build or game validation ran.

## Legacy native recipe-slot translation

A160 — Integration issue (I), not a new Android bug. Published native checkpoints identify each acquired effect by content key, application index and slot, then require exact trigger/action types. Replacing a recipe can therefore break restoration even when its Android behavior is constructed correctly. Holy Arts changes from three initial/two repeat native slots to one source producer; Death Rounds changes from one native buff action to no effect. Glare and Focal Point have different original first/repeat layouts. These are known data-layout changes, not grounds for changing Android behavior.

The local reader now translates exact original layouts for71 named Windows powerup recipes after the existing typed runtime conversions have extracted their counters/remaining times. Acquisitions retain their recorded key/application ownership. Already-current layouts keep their saved fields; known obsolete slots become the actual replacement action's instant/typed trigger slot or Death Rounds' empty layout. Two known earlier Gale/Regeneration periodic adapters also translate to their source attachment owners, whose clocks are not saved. Unknown layouts remain untouched for the existing strict restore reader. The conversion does not equip perks, execute actions or rewrite the owned file. It does not invent omitted Android runtime counters or recover unknown unlimited native grants.

Source boundaries: the hash-bound original Windows inventory supplies each first/stacked trigger/action layout; native ApplyStack uses stackedEffects when nonempty and otherwise effects. The published v1.0.0 scope writer supplies acquisition/slot identity. Retained input examples include native Frost Mage and already-converted Glare/Holy Arts/Shatter/Elements records; the examples are read-only layout evidence, not an executed migration or gameplay result. Private evidence: legacy-recipe-identity-source/{recipe-layouts.json,retained-recipe-records.json,source-contract.json}.

This selected identity translation is locally implemented, uncompiled/unverified. Legacy aggregate stat attribution, other old provider/effect variants and current/legacy/native/Custom behavior remain internal P2-G3 acceptance blockers; G1/G2 coverage remains open. The final anomaly review stays OPEN and precedes any whole-port build/game validation. No Android bug was corrected and no continuation acceptance is claimed.

## Timed source producers and their checkpoint ownership

A161 — Gameplay rule/quirk/integration issue (G/Q/I), not a newly identified Android bug. GaleOnLastAmmo's name does not establish an ammo/reload listener. Its actual Start copies OnSpawninterval=2 into a remaining-time field; Update subtracts deltaTime, creates one Gale when remaining<=0, then assigns the full interval. Overshoot is discarded, and each attached helper owns its countdown. The previous Windows PeriodicTrigger adapter retained coroutine timing rather than this frame-driven owner.

Regeneration's original PF_DropHPOverTime uses DropHPOverTime.timeToDrop=90. Start registers the heart prefab and calls InvokeRepeating with both initial delay and period90. DropHP supplies its helper position before pooled activation; OnDestroy cancels invokes. The port now attaches native DropPrefabOverTime per pick and changes these source-owner Start/drop boundaries; its matching native CancelInvoke teardown remains. The earlier filter-only native periodic recipe and its historical first-drop checks do not establish this changed lifecycle.

Both are AttachPrefabPowerup recipes, and neither helper has a branch in that powerup's runtime-value dispatcher. They therefore reconstruct fresh clocks on source Continue. The local actions defer their first Start until synchronous runtime/ammo restoration finishes, through the existing source-start queue; they do not store a new phase field. Legacy original Gale's two effect records and Regeneration's instant-plus-periodic records, plus the two known earlier periodic adapters, translate to instant source-owner actions. Energized's three original OnThunder records become one random-thunder/action owner; Hellspawns/Thunderspawns use the already implemented typed surviving-summon-hurt trigger identities. Obsolete native counters are not invented as source saved progress.

Private evidence: periodic-producers-save-source/game-flows-arm64.json (four Gale methods, Start0x1899bb0/Update0x1899bbc/OnSpawn0x1899c00), regeneration/game-flows-arm64.json (four DropHPOverTime methods, Start0x1985e44/DropHP0x1985f10/OnDestroy0x1985f08), source-contract.json, hash-bound original inventories, retained native DropPrefabOverTime IL and existing ordered-source attachment dispatcher evidence. Retained old input layouts are examples, not executed migrations or gameplay acceptance.

Current code is uncompiled/unverified. Actual initial/repeated acquisition, frame cadence, pickup activation/teardown, source/legacy/same/fresh-process Continue and Custom/native interactions remain required whole-port acceptance. Legacy aggregate stat attribution, other source/API/provider gaps and final delivery remain internal P2-G1/G2/G3 work. Final pre-build anomaly review stays OPEN. No independent Android fix, build or game validation occurred.

## Known legacy character-stat contributions

A162 — Integration issue (I), not an Android bug. The fresh replay applies Android's Hina Enhanced Shadow +25% global Summon Damage and Lilith Necromancer +20% per application (A50), but legacy restoration subsequently assigns its saved raw StatMod fields. The original Windows assets have no direct statChanges for either key, and the earlier tracked writer/source recipes lack both additions. Copying that prior total therefore removes the new contribution even though the helper/action has been reconstructed.

The local read conversion now adds those known missing contributions to the legacy aggregate target for each recorded application, including extra applications already journaled by Specialize and each saved Custom perk scale. It retains other bonuses and does not invent Feed/Ritual/hurt/heal history. The optional characterSummonBonuses data field marks a translated in-memory state; new aggregate captures write true because their totals already contain the source contribution. Subsequent reads therefore do not add it again. Reading preserves the owned file. Fresh schema7 bypasses this legacy conversion and continues using actual source perk replay.

Native Powerup.ApplyStats distinguishes flat additions, positive additive bonuses and negative multiplicative reductions. This selected migration is confined to the two established positive additions; it does not subtract displayed percentages, divide unknown/zero factors, attribute arbitrary totals to specific helpers or claim other old stat recipes are reconciled. The legacy raw-field/notification path and other lost-history attribution still require G3 work.

Private evidence: legacy-stat-attribution-source/source-contract.json binds the two original Android/Windows recipes, earlier tracked AndroidStatRecipes blob at the current base commit, native ApplyStats/StatMod IL and current changed source. No retained legacy saved-run input with either named acquisition was located, and no migration or player execution was performed. Actual repeated/current/legacy/source/Custom continuation, marker resave/reload behavior and observer integration remain whole-port acceptance work. P2 remains ACTIVE; G1/G2/G3 and the final pre-build review remain OPEN. No build/game validation or independent Android fix ran.

## Stat-notification producer investigation

- A123 — Best Friends' named stat notification has no publisher at the inspected binding boundary. EventStatChange is a static string on StatPowerup, not the similarly named per-instance StatMod.ChangedEvent. The literal relocation is 0x39f5bb8; the StatPowerup TypeInfo relocation is 0x39f5868. A register-write-aware scan across every file-backed executable section finds only constructors requiring class initialization, the static initializer that stores the string, and SummonAttackCDEffect.Start reading it to call AddObserver. Full direct-callee inspection of those reference owners finds no PostNotification. The three StatMod mutators invoke their ChangedEvent delegates; the reviewed StatsHolder constructor does not install a global forwarding event. StatPowerup.Apply does not publish the named event, and Powerup.ApplyAndNotify publishes the distinct Powerup.AppliedNotifcation name, initialized independently at 0x198d27c. Scanning every decompressed Unity object's raw payload for UTF8/UTF16 EventStatChange finds no serialized literal binding. Consequently the reviewed acquisition/stat-change routes do not establish an automatic Best Friends factor update. The port retains the source held factor and named listener; no publisher or ChangedEvent forwarding is invented. Its existing explicit-notification fixtures remain consumer-only evidence. This does not prove arbitrary computed/reflection references impossible or establish an Android runtime failure: the instruction scan covers aligned ADRP/ADD/MOV/LDR propagation within31 instructions and containing metadata ranges, with volatile registers cleared on calls. Remaining broader source coverage stays P2-G2 work.

Private A123 evidence: stat-event-field-source/game-flows-arm64.json, field-references-and-serialized-literals.json and reference-owner-callees.json; stat-event-owner-source/game-flows-arm64.json; stat-event-emitter-boundaries-source/game-flows-arm64.json; retained stat-notification-producer-source and ghost-stat-observers exports. These refine the earlier broad, register-clobber-prone candidate scan rather than treating its false candidates as publishers. No production code change, runtime acceptance run or balance correction was made by this investigation.

# Evidence and coverage note

This ledger retains stable anomaly IDs and bounded source addresses. References to evidence/ and ledger/ identify private reproducible research records, not bundled game assets or publicly accessible downloads. Native run numbers identify isolated copied-player fixtures with explicit seeded preconditions. They do not claim full unassisted balance playtests. Source-shape differences alone do not establish method equivalence. The required target is Android behavior, not a judgment about whether it is better balanced. All Android gameplay differences are required parity work.

# Gameplay parity and anomalies

Compared Steam build 12679152 and the supplied Android 8.6 package. Android gameplay parity is mandatory, including balance and anomalous behavior. The requested rune economy remains a separate user override. A serialized difference is evidence of different data, not proof that Windows is broken or that Android is better balanced.
