## Required parity implementation

Culling, Mini Clip, Giant and Tomes are implemented at their recorded verified boundaries. All remaining Android gameplay differences below are mandatory; investigation and verification resolve the target behavior without another approval or review gate.

# Additional findings from the burn investigation

- A78 — Repeated Glare reaches a different attachment contract. The Android Glare asset (sharedassets1 ID3625) calls AttachPrefabPowerup.Apply0x189c488, instantiating another PF_AttachVisionDamage with independent15 damage, multiplier1, interval1 and no hit flag (helper ID3789). Windows' stackedEffects instead adds a flat25 Glare DamageBuff. Android VisionPowerup.Apply0x18a3a20 only upgrades currently attached helpers, so a later Glare does not inherit prior Intense Glare, Saccade or Sight Magic. Both original prefab hierarchies contain only Transform and AttachVisionDamage; reconstruction does not remove hidden artwork or physics. Source Update emits actor IDs on an unscoped impact channel; the native OnHitTrigger requires the owning player/GameObject payload, which the port explicitly adapts. This is implemented, not postponed or an optional balance change. Android Endless offer eligibility is still separately required under A67.

P2 Glare verification: pre-port S4/run105 fails source geometry/damage. Acquisition106 verifies the four actual menu upgrades and repeated applications, strict pooled-center radius from live vision, duplicate-collider deduplication, unpooled/outside-center exclusion, typed30 damage/0.5-second pulse, and ordered producer state. A repeated Glare first deals15 with its own one-second clock and no hit event, while the older helper retains45/0.5 and Sight Magic. Later upgrades yield60+30, then actual Might changes them independently to58+28. Real Sight Magic dispatch reaches native Pyro Mage and the source burn backend; the fixture seeds only the native hit callback's probability boundary. Save captures separate clock values; fresh-process Continue107 restores both and all ordered upgrades, repeats geometry/damage/burn checks and clears the owners on an original restart. Wind checkpoint regression108 passes against the same production binary after the added typed provider. Native originals, vision graphics and controls remain owned by the game. G1/G2/G3 remain unresolved blockers to full parity/delivery; P2 stays ACTIVE, with no intermediate deployment or release.

- A12 — Burn lifetime changed materially between platforms. Confirmed static code: Windows independently expires each additive application after its own duration; Android accumulates damage in a shared BurnBuff and refreshes its remaining tick count on reapplication. Android's shipped level2 scene caps burn damage at 1000 per tick before Health processing, overriding the constructor's 100. Impact: copying only burn percentages or assuming a pure optimization would change sustained-fire balance.   A13 separately corrects proven query truncation. Evidence: evidence/fire-behavior/README.md and fire-arm64.json.
- A13 — Confirmed reachable Combustion field query truncation, corrected. Native Flame Cannon → level-20 Combustion choice → three real Health kills spawns PF_BurningField/PersistentBurn (component 5990). S3/run-30 verifies five live test enemies all overlap the query, yet only one receives a burn: the native two-collider buffer truncates six collider results. Android PeriodicallyBurnArea uses a growable ID list and GetInRadius scans all registered transforms without this cap. src/BurnAreaCoverage.cs replaces only the Windows physics query with a dynamically enlarged, component-owned reusable buffer; the native timer and Burn loop remain intact. S3/run-32 burns all five known enemies on the first pass, retains damage 3 / interval 0.5 seconds and exits the native scene without exceptions. Collider-overlap geometry and Windows stacking/lifetimes are retained. Earlier runs 28/29 exposed fixture assumptions about pool parenting / distinct targets per collider; run-31 used the previous binary after a compile failure and is not corrected-code evidence. The runner now rejects binaries older than compiled source. Source: evidence/fire-behavior/README.md, fire-arm64.json and exact native asset IDs.

- A14 — Confirmed native pooled-summon teardown defect. Native Katana/Magic Bow scene exit in evidence/S3/run-13 fails with SeekerWeapon.OnDestroy; run-14's bounded reproduction logs unused PF_MagicSword pool members inactive with Summon.stats null immediately before each exception. Summon.Start assigns stats and calls Init, which subscribes to the attack-speed event; unused pool members never run Start, yet OnDestroy dereferences the property to unsubscribe. src/SummonLifecycle.cs skips only teardown of never-initialized members and retains original unsubscribe for initialized instances. run-15 verifies native scene exit with both one initialized and 29 unused members, no exceptions. Android 8.6 OnDestroy still calls get_summonAtkSpdMod, but that getter uses the global current player; its native lifetime ownership must be reconciled. Source-linked ARM ranges: evidence/S3/lifecycle-arm64.json/.txt. No combat or balance values changed.

- A15 — Historical Windows choice-generation hazard and mod-only guards. Windows GetRandom uses rejection sampling until it finds the requested number of distinct eligible choices, even if fewer exist; run-24 demonstrated that mechanism with an invalid fixture pool. The older mod reduced choice slots and skipped exhausted level-up/chest transitions, verified in run-37's seeded Windows offers. That result establishes the older mod's behavior, not Android parity, naturally reached exhaustion or an Android runtime freeze. Android's fallback selection/count/removal paths and upstream callers require complete reconciliation. A95 supersedes the earlier endorsement of those extra guards; they are not an authorized exception to the required Android behavior.

## Existing-perk behavior differences found in the native-body audit

- A44 — Splinter changes inherited projectile size and its activation limiter. Windows' radial action multiplies the gun recipe size by 0.5 and limits that action to three activations per frame. Android ShootBulletsOnDeath's coroutine (0x198923c) assigns recipe size=0.5 outright; its OnDeath (0x19890b0) and coroutine use one static currentBullets counter shared across instances, incremented/decremented around yielding work. The base three-projectile / 15%-damage recipe remains.

  Native355/356 now verifies two actual source attachments, real Curse/Summon/Bullet deaths with no Enemy-tag requirement and a shared2/1/0 coroutine admission sequence. Three admitted coroutines emit6/3/0 shots at their respective death positions; their three-shot bursts retain source120-degree spacing, absolute size0.5 versus an actual Big Shot recipe1.4,15% damage,35% speed and10% knockback. Count3 remains during the two final yields then returns0. An actual source projectile's physical kill starts both helpers again. Native save captures six live shots after coroutines finish; exact source Continue omits world shots/coroutine phase and rebuilds two owners. Ordinary restart's actual NPC death produces no old burst. Fixture target/death positioning, shot parking and stopped scheduler are explicit; no natural trajectory/full-run claim. Selected legacy/Custom and delivered boundaries remain open. No source behavior is corrected.

- A43 — Android Shatter loses the documented frozen-target guard in the inspected build. Windows ShatterFrozenAction.Activate checks FreezeSystem.IsFrozen before spawning its explosion. Android IceShatterOnDeath.Start (0x1899c90) subscribes to Health.DeathEvent, and OnDeath (0x1899ea0) spawns/sets percentage damage without checking frozen state. Health.Death (0x197dea4) posts that event for any notified non-player death, before pool release; it has no freeze-only filter. Static consequence: the source handler also processes unfrozen enemy deaths, unlike Windows' documented effect.  The source path is now reconstructed in Windows: acquisition167/Continue169 verify actual unfrozen and untagged Health deaths, two independently floored7-damage Bullet contacts without bullet-hit procs, a fixture with50 active effects, half-second expiry/LIFO reuse and saved Might. That fixture did not establish the source cap: A87 supersedes the earlier50-cap interpretation with the source51-active boundary; the corrected current pool remains unverified. Source affix interactions remain A67 work. This is source-bound Windows verification, not an Android runtime playtest. A39 records repeat strength changes.

- A39 — Several rewritten repeat effects no longer use Windows' distinct first/stack recipes. Windows Frostbite has 15% ordinary-enemy damage initially and 10% per extra pick; Shatter has 7% initially and 5% per extra pick. Android attaches another helper with the original 15% / 7% fields on each repeated application. Assassin's Windows stacked threshold is 25% versus the initial 20%; Android helper instances retain autoKillPercent=0.2 and use a projectile-impact callback. Ghost Wizard assigns GhostFriend's bulletEffect=true / BurnDameage=6 on Android, rather than adding another Windows summon-hit trigger/action pair. Ghost Wizard's assignment, later-pristine-actor exclusion, per-shot burn-before-bullet-proc adapter and pooled reset now pass actual acquisition158/fresh-process Continue159, including actual Pyro Mage contributions6 then9, one player-owned impact, mixed saved actors and clean restart. The native chance roll and small pool are explicit fixture preconditions; no probability-frequency or whole-projectile-engine claim is made. Frostbite/Shatter/Assassin acquisition167/Continue169 now pass independent full-strength owners, real freeze/death/native gun impacts and ordered restoration. Frostbite preserves a separate source Freze category through a reserved native damage value, with independently floored15%/1% contributions and signed Might. Assassin adds source10% projectile speed per pick, holds20% on repeats, includes real EnemyChampion targets and ignores damage without impact. The native impact payload adapter does not establish complete source projectile producer/trigger/continuous-collision parity; those and affix consumers remain mandatory work. Controlled full-run balance measurements are separate from implementing them.
- A40 — Gale/Eye of the Storm change geometry despite similar descriptions. Windows Aero Magic uses a two-second PeriodicTrigger with an already-correct current suffixed description; its throw action uses an 80-degree total spread. Android GaleSystem.Create (0x19736f0) chooses an integer angle from -60 to 60 around ShootDir and spawns PF_Gale at player position. Eye of the Storm enables DurationDamageBuff on that moving PF_Gale: sharedassets2 ID 231 specifies radius 3 / two-second ticks / 2x Gale damage, and Update (0x1985f78) queries around its own transform. Windows' periodic AreaTargetAction is centered on the player with range 5.  The unused older description key is stale, but no current tooltip correction is required.

- A77 — The source Gale prefab and contact lifecycle differ beyond A40 geometry. Original-pointer Android PF_Gale has TimeToLive4.5, PersistentHarm with a duplicate-preserving actor-ID list and no Projectile component; Windows has lifetime10, a HashSet of contacts and additional Projectile callbacks. PersistentHarm.OnCollisionEnter2D0x1989e20/OnCollisionExit2D0x198a008/Update0x198a1b0 adds/removes one matching contact, ticks every0.5 seconds, and retains its timer/list across pooled reuse. GaleSystem.Create captures floored damage and Eye-enabled state per spawn. DurationDamageBuff.Update0x1985f78 instead reads live GaleSystem damage for each two-second pulse, doubles it, and queries strict monster-pool centers within3 of the moving Gale. Neither timer resets on enable/disable. WindbornePowerup.Apply0x189f820 adds movement every acquisition but AddDamageStatType0x1973aa8 registers the scaling type only once. EyeOfTheStormPowerup.Apply0x189f690 adds5 before scaling and enables the area only on subsequent casts; GaleDamagePowerup.Apply0x189f764 adds15 before scaling. The source LIFO release order in A76 also affects these retained clocks. These behaviors are implemented through an owned cloned template, source wind owner/contact component, and the existing native input, graphics, sound, TimeToLive and checkpoint effect lifecycle. The original Windows template is preserved.

P2 wind verification: S4/run-98 reproduces the old ten-second lifetime. Acquisition101 and fresh-process Continue103 verify actual native choices/repeats, original-template preservation, player-origin speed3/integer59-degree casts, damage46 before Haste versus54 after it, duplicate contact hits using captured46, moving Eye108 with an outside-center overlapping collider excluded, per-spawn Eye state, LIFO/timer retention and actual4.5-second expiry. Repeat Windborne/Mastery produces82; after the actual Might tome, Continue contacts deal80 each and live Eye162. Continue restores the saved producer deadline and clears all source wind owners on an original restart. Run102 is a fixture-reader failure; using the checkpoint's actual DataContract reader fixes it without a production change. Existing live effect objects/pool contents remain outside the semantic checkpoint contract. Other P2 gameplay/coverage/delivery blockers remain required; no deployment or release.

Affected regressions: S2/19 acquires Aero Mastery through the native menu on the actual Gale character and verifies that the source wind bonus does not affect her separate Bullet-stat slash's six-damage ticks; existing ability/exclusive/animation/native-input/cleanup/original-restart checks pass. S2/18 stops at an old fixture assumption that new content is automatically owned; the fixture now seeds only its required earned content in the isolated profile before using the real native locks. Current lightning Continue104 passes both source overloads, Mastery/Elements/Might, real collisions, LIFO state, per-actor Energized and original restart using the current production binary. The shared pool helper now serves these two actual effect owners, and the signed-health bridge lives at its existing damage boundary; there is no global pool replacement or additional checkpointed world state.
- A41 — Electro Mage's target selection changes. Both recipes retain one 22-damage lightning strike every two shots. Windows ThunderNearCursorAction selects the enemy closest to the shooting cursor; Android ThunderOnAttack.Attack (0x189bb80) chooses a random enemy in its source 7x4 region around the player's transform. This is required gameplay parity, implemented locally and unverified; native Windows input/aim remains a separate explicit requirement.
- A42 — Sword and Shield's completion boundary differs. Windows SmiteHolyShieldCDR adds one second to an inactive shield's elapsed recovery timer. Android's corresponding callback (0x18a3c20) subtracts one from the scheduled apply time through ReduceApplyTime (0x19621e0), but if it crosses the current game clock it clamps the next apply to current time +1 second.

- A38 — Summon growth/composition changes beyond base recipe values. Windows Trained Dragon adds +0.1 to each summon instance's attackSpeedMod every minute; Android BuffSummonAttackSpeedOverTime.Update (0x1898754) divides its captured summon cooldown by 1.1 per tick, producing repeated multiplicative growth rather than Windows' additive rate bonus. Windows Scythe's BulletDamage/MoveSpeed damage modifiers share the original incoming value in NotifyModifiers; Android WeaponSummon.OnCollisionEnter2D (0x19a3e70) successively multiplies its summon-stat damage by enabled movement/bullet factors using static HashSet membership. Repeated acquisition can add more Windows modifiers while Android membership stays idempotent. Soul Powered similarly moves its current-soul-heart bonus into the BulletDamage StatMod rather than Windows' downstream damage modifier.

- A36 — Confirmed Generator is nonfunctional in the shipped Windows prefab, corrected. Original Generator (sharedassets1 ID 856) attaches PF_FireRateBuffOnAmmoGain (GameObject 363 / Transform 469), containing only a Transform and no children. Run-42 equips its actual native prerequisite chain and Generator, observes that empty object, then verifies native ammo gain produces no fire-rate bonus. A separate fixture attachment of the compiled Windows FireRateBuffOnGainAmmo helper with Android's +35% / two-second recipe works once but fails after expiry because BuffCR retains its expired timer. Android FireRateBuffOnAmmoGain.ApplyBuff (0x1971d44) restarts its own duration correctly. src/GeneratorBuff.cs restores the existing native Windows helper on Generator's actual effect object and resets its timer only on a new inactive activation of that named Generator helper. Run-44 verifies gain, refresh without self-stacking, independent two-second expiry, subsequent activation, all three finite native Endless picks, active-buff native death/title and an unchanged original restart. Run-43 is a failed implementation: its postfix incorrectly declared a GameObject argument for Powerup.Apply(PlayerController), causing a Unity native crash; the actual native signature was corrected before run-44. It is not acceptance evidence. No other perk's timer or bonus is changed.
- A37 — Existing summon base tables include further balance changes. Dragon's ShootingSummon source changes from Windows base damage 20 / cooldown 2 seconds (sharedassets1 ID 949) to Android 10 / 3 seconds (ID 4280), retaining three projectiles; both egg assets specify 180 seconds to hatch. Scythe's OrbitalWeapon baseRotationSpeed changes from -300 (Windows ID 895) to -160 (Android ID 3876), retaining base damage 40. Android GetProjectileRecipe (0x19a1734) consumes the source summon base damage; OnSummonAtkSpdChange (0x19a08b8) consumes the source base rotation speed.  These are source recipe differences, not a measured whole-build DPS comparison or authorization for a nerf.

- A33 — Kill Clip broadens the death-event filter. Windows ReloadRateUpOnKill.OnDeath checks the exact Enemy tag before adding its 5% reload-rate stack; Android OnDeath (0x1861294) casts the sender to Health but does not check that tag before adding the same bonus and stack count. Both reset stacks on reload.
- A34 — Siege changes ammo conservation into ammo gain. Windows RefundAmmoWhileStillBuff can veto native ammo consumption with 40% probability while stationary, sampled around fixed updates. Android RefundAmmoWhenStanding.Start (0x189aaa8) subscribes to gun OnShoot; ChangeToRefundAmmo (0x189ac64) uses movement measured in Update, rolls the same probability and calls GainAmmo(1). Ammo gain notifications, magazine caps and effects that react to gained ammo can therefore differ.
- A35 — Regeneration changes the first pickup's timing. Windows' perk has an InstantTrigger to spawn one native red-heart pickup, followed by a ninety-second periodic spawn. Android DropHPOverTime.Start (0x1985e44) schedules InvokeRepeating with initial delay and interval both equal to source timeToDrop=90; there is no immediate DropHP call in that method. Both use pickups rather than the direct grants in A20.

- A31 — Vision's transform cap differs from its raw stat. Windows StatModTransformScale on PlayerVision (level1 ID 1934) clamps visual scale to 2.5 while retaining the underlying VisionRange stat. Android StatPowerup.Apply (0x189ca60, vision branch 0x189cd28) writes Vector3.one * VisionRange.Modify(1) without that clamp in the inspected branch; its Glare radius also reads the stat directly (A26). Pickup/character transform branches similarly read their semantic stats, whose Windows maxScale is infinite.
- A32 — Hellspawns adds a boss restriction in the Android callback. Windows SummonOnHitTrigger applies the configured burn to a matching summoned hit without a boss filter. Android HellspawnsPowerup.onHurt (0x18a22cc) checks the configured summon damage category and returns when Health.CreatureType equals 2, defined as BossMonster by the supplied EnumCreature (TypeDefIndex 4812). The remaining callback applies source burnDamage=3.  Thunderspawns' separately inspected callback does not contain that same boss check.

- A30 — Death Rounds moves and broadens its flat damage addition. Windows' native DamageBuff adds +10 after Bullet damage processing. Android FlatDamageUp.Start (0x1986394) instead adds the source damage=10 to both BulletDamage and SummonDamage StatMod flat bonuses (semantic enum values 3 and 4). Those flat bonuses then participate in their respective native stat consumers and multiplier ordering.

- A26 — Glare's rewritten consumers are not a drop-in replacement. Android AttachVisionDamage.Update (0x1897c6c) scans a radius derived from VisionRange.Modify(2), deals ceil(damage * damageMultiplie), and optionally posts the projectile impact notification. Its source baseline is 15 damage / one second; Intense Glare adds 2-1 to the multiplier, so ordinary unmodified first-pick output matches Windows' 15+15. Saccade instead assigns secondsPerTick=0.5 on every application; the Windows serialized actions encode +1 tick speed first and +0.5 on repeats. Those recipe values alone do not establish actual repeated runtime cadence: pre-port native run105 already observes0.5 after two picks, but fails the source geometry/damage boundary. Windows' collider-based vision damage is replaced by the source strict-center monster-pool query. Source application order, independent clocks, typed damage and native hit consumers are integrated and verified below.
- A27 — Reflex changes which movement growth becomes dodge. Windows ModDodgeByMoveSpeedBuff multiplies the current dodge calculation by MoveSpeed.Modify(1). Android ModDodgeByMoveSpeedPowerup.Apply (0x18a0068) records the movement factor after its initial +15% movement stat, then listens for later changes. UpdateDogeByMoveSpeed (0x18a016c) adds subsequent movement-factor deltas to the Dodge stat until its source MaxChangeToValue=0.1 is reached.
- A28 — Best Friends changes attack-speed composition. Windows applies FireRate.ModifyInverse(1) to the GhostFriend cooldown calculation. Android BestFriendsPowerup.Apply (0x198cdd4) installs SummonAttackCDEffect; OnStateChange (0x192f73c) starts at 1 and adds each selected StatMod.Modify(1) to attackEffect. AttackingSummon.get_finalAttackCooldown (0x199fc5c) divides summon-stat-adjusted cooldown by that factor. With one FireRate selection and factor 1.1, the isolated Android factor is 2.1 versus Windows 1.1, before other modifiers.

A28 source-consumer integration: Start0x192f56c subscribes to EventStatChange without invoking the callback. InitStateType0x192f618 deduplicates the selected FireRate type without recomputing. The port retains its held factor until that actual notification, then assigns1+FireRate.Modify(1), including repeats and independent Ghost owners. Acquisition151/Continue152 verify the natural10% FireRate grant and exact consumer behavior under explicitly seeded named notifications. Those dispatches do not prove an Android gameplay emitter. The all-executable-section metadata-slot scan finds listener/initializer/constructor candidates, with indirect-reference limitations; producer/reachability remains required G2 investigation. No invented producer or continuous ChangedEvent recalculation is substituted.
- A29 — Magic Lens changes damage composition and introduces a size clamp. Windows BulletEnhanceSummon.ModifyProjectile adds summon-modified gun damage * source 0.3 * overallMultiplier to the current projectile damage. Android OnProjectile (0x199fe88) multiplies the current projectile damage, after summon stat adjustment, by source damageMultiplier=1.3 and overallMultiplier, then clamps its resulting size to 0..5. Those can match a simple unmodified first hit but differ for projectile-specific damage, summon bonuses and repeated lens crossings. Focal Point's source Apply (0x189ea2c) also applies half-size/+1 overall each time, whereas Windows stackedEffects add only +0.5 overall after the first pick. 3→1.3 value.

- A22 — Soul Knight changes the meaning of its damage growth. Windows SoulKnight (sharedassets1 ID 811) subscribes to OnSHPGainedTrigger and adds a permanent +15 Spear damage buff per gain event, also spawning a soul-heart pickup. PlayerHealth.shp invokes onGainedSHP when the requested shield value increases, before clamping. Android SoulKnightPowerup.Apply (0x18a1ae0, ID 3568) assigns static extraDamage=15; StabbingSummon.OnCollisionEnter2D (0x19a2660) multiplies it by currently held ShieldHp. Losing shields therefore removes Android's corresponding damage, unlike Windows' accumulated gains. Both descriptions still refer to gaining soul hearts.  Native multi-heart assignment remains one Windows gain event, not one event per heart.

A22 integration: acquisition137/Continue138 now reproduce the Android current-soul-heart coefficient, including assignment of multiple hearts, complete loss, repeat picks assigning15, and inheritance by later-created spears. The native gain-history buff and pickup action are replaced. Source Holy Spear adds10 per application; collision floors summonStat.Modify(base20)+maxHP*HolyCoefficient+currentSoulHP*SoulCoefficient before the signed health modifier stage. Actual collisions verify injury independence, Death Rounds and Might composition. Old native-effect checkpoint compatibility remains required G3 work.
- A23 — Energized replaces deterministic cadence with randomness. Windows' OnThunderTrigger counts five hits before restoring three ammo. Android AmmoOnThunder (sharedassets1 ID 3900), OnThunderHit (0x1897770), draws Random.Range(0,1) against chanceToActivate=0.2 on each hit before the same GainAmmo(3). Equal long-run expectation does not mean identical timing, variance or build reliability.
- A24 — Justice changes the counted event and explicit maximum assignment. Android GainMaxHPOnSmiteKill (sharedassets1 ID4607), OnSmiteKill0x195fc38, counts500 SmiteKillNotification events per increment and stops after three increments per helper. Source Smite posts that notification even for surviving/protected captured targets. Windows Justice already has limitActivations=true/limit3 around its500-kill OnKillTrigger: the earlier claim of no native per-perk cap was incorrect. Android adds one flat MaxHP stat and explicitly assigns min(20,floor(stat.Modify(character.startHP))); its native-compatible helper is reconstructed per application. Suppress only Windows' automatic health listener while the explicit source assignment runs, preserving native stat notifications without an intermediate uncapped heal. The saved actual maximum/injury also remains authoritative during final stat restoration. Acquisition128 and fresh-process Continue130 verify separate owners, source event counting, capped injury, partial/growth counters, ordinary uncapped Vitality and original restart. Legacy inline-Justice checkpoint conversion remains required G3 work.
- A25 — Fresh Clip changes bonus strength and overlapping reloads. Windows sharedassets1 ID 829 uses a one-second TemporaryStatBuff with +50% BulletDamage. Android FreshClip ID 3587 attaches PF_DamageUpOnReload / DamageUpOnReload (ID 4897) with damageBonus=0.3 for one second. OnReload (0x1860f60) adds the bonus only while its timer is inactive and then refreshes that timer; Update (0x18610f0) removes it once at expiry. Windows independently attached temporary buffs can overlap. Anger Point likewise changes overlapping +70% damage/fire-rate thirty-second buffs to refresh: FireRateOnHurt.OnHurt (0x18998a0) stops the existing coroutine, removes its bonus and starts a fresh one.

These are static source findings, not validated balance changes or proof that one platform is better balanced. Android values/semantics are required under the corrected M7; the earlier instruction to retain Windows behavior is superseded. Source IDs are in active-powerup-comparison.json; exact bounded ARM ranges and ELF digests are in evidence/S3/powerups-arm64.json, powerup-classes-arm64.json and powerup-backends-arm64.json.

- A16 — Player red-health cap/rounding differs. Windows StatModHealth (level1 ID 1870) caps derived max HP at 20, and PlayerHealth.maxHP also clamps red HP plus soul-heart capacity to 20. Android StatPowerup.Apply (0x189ca60) floors its HP stat result with a minimum of one, then Health.set_maxHP (0x197b394) assigns it without that 20 cap in the inspected path. This changes extreme health builds and can affect fractional modifiers. The source cap/rounding is required parity; the current shared-health batch remains uncompiled/unverified, and A24's separate Justice assignment cap must not be generalized to ordinary HP upgrades.
- A17 — Stalwart Shield changes recovery arithmetic. Both source shields start at 120 seconds (Windows sharedassets0 ID 6171; Android ID 3829). Windows HolyShieldCDRAction adds 1 to cooldownRate, giving 120/(1+n) absent other modifiers. Android's source cdr=60 goes through PreventDamage.ReduceCD (0x196229c); get_realCooldownTime (0x19622dc) is max(10, cooldownTime-reduceTime). With n Stalwart picks alone that gives 60, 10, 10 seconds versus Windows 60, 40, 30. Windows already rechecks finalCooldown during its active recovery coroutine; there is no basis to claim that it lacks live cooldown updates.
- A18 — Several specialized rewrites change modifier placement/form. GaleDamagePowerup adds 15 to a native StatMod before GaleSystem.get_Damage (0x197395c) applies its enabled movement stat multipliers; Windows adds Aero Mastery's 15 through DamageBuff/AddValueModifier alongside MultiplyStatToDamage using the original damage argument. For the isolated raw 20-base, +15, 2x-stat recipe this is 70 versus 55 before other processing. Electro Mastery replaces Windows' percentage damage/AoE modifiers with a flat +12 thunder modifier and 1.75x AoE in ThunderDamageUp.Apply (0x18a2a58). Intense Glare adds1 to each currently attached Glare producer's multiplier rather than a flat damage modifier; the shipped base15 makes ordinary first-pick damage30. Electro's source placement is integrated with actual Elements/Might acquisitions; Aero's flat-before-movement placement and unique Windborne provider pass repeated acquisitions/Continue (A77). Glare's actual consumers, repeat attachment and upgrade ordering now pass native acquisition/Continue (A26/A78).
- A19 — Numeric interaction candidates extend beyond the earlier stat-list comparison. Thunderspawns serializes hit chance 0.30 Windows / 0.15 Android, with source damage 22 retained. Pulsing Summons' area range is 2 Windows / 3 Android. Soul Link's area range is 15 Windows / 3 Android; its 80% ordinary-enemy percentage remains serialized. Native consumer/geometry and champion handling still determine net behavior.
- A20 — Heart delivery changes from pickups to immediate health/shield grants. Windows Bloodsuckers and Soul Drain spawn their native heart/soul-heart pickup after 500 summon kills. Android KillEffectPowerup.OnKill (0x18a03a0) directly calls HPChange or set_ShieldHp after the same threshold. Soul Conversion (renamed Soul Expand) similarly replaces a spawned soul-heart pickup with a direct shield-heart grant alongside capacity. This changes collection timing, full-health waste and pickup interactions.
- A21 — Confirmed Holy Spear tooltip/behavior defect, corrected. Both shipped descriptions specify +10 damage per maximum HP. Windows HolySpear (sharedassets1 ID 808) instead attaches AddHPToDamageMod, which reads current HP. Android HolySpearPowerup.Apply (0x18a1944) supplies 10; StabbingSummon.OnCollisionEnter2D (0x19a2660) multiplies it by Health.maxHP. Native run-39 reproduces 60 damage at 4/4 HP, 50 at 3/4, and 60 after Vitality at 4/5. src/HolySpearMaximumHealth.cs replaces only this perk's Spear damage modifier with an equivalent additive modifier reading maximum HP; other HP modifiers and damage ordering are unchanged. Runs 40/41 verify 60 at both 4/4 and 3/4, 70 at 4/5, then 120/170 after the second/third finite Endless picks; the native pool removes the exhausted perk. Native death/title and original Shana/Revolver restart pass. The fixture positions an initialized native Spear for real physics collisions while disabling autonomous attack updates; it verifies native hit damage and lifecycle, not targeting AI.


- A45 — Holy Arts repeat strength/timing differs. Windows inline HolyArts sharedassets1 effects deal base 20 at multiplier 1 initially, 0.5 for repeated picks, with separate delayed visuals/damage. Android PF_SmitePassive/SmitePassive ID4617 attaches another full 20-damage producer per pick; SmiteCR (0x1960730) captures radius-four colliders, displays effects, then applies the full modifier-adjusted base. A zero count of Windows SmitePassive components is expected because Windows uses AreaTargetAction/DealDamageAction, not a missing helper defect.
- A46 — Dragon Bond changes damage classification/composition. Windows AddSummonDamageToDamageMod adds Dragon baseDamage*0.1 to Bullet damage; Android DragonBonusDamage (0x1985990/0x1985c38) floors that base-only amount and issues a separate projectile-impact HPChange with Summon damage. Neither inspected getter uses the fully summon-stat-adjusted Dragon damage.
- A47 — Magic Spear targeting changes from a radial eligibility check to a square query. Windows StabbingSummon.Attack queries 9x6, then rejects a chosen target farther than range=5. Android Attack (0x19a28cc) queries +/-range on each axis without the radial rejection and extends a stab to the range endpoint in that direction. Both base recipes are two spears, damage20, cooldown1.3, range5. Android targeting is now implemented: acquisition137 verifies a pooled target at(4.5,4.5), exclusion of a boundary-center target at5 and a tagged unpooled actor, plus the complete actual native outbound/return tween. Continue138 restores the independent clocks/effects and complete persistent spear group, including capture while detached.

A40 tooltip false-positive correction: the actual aero_magic_description_2 already says two seconds / 20 damage / 0.5-second ticks. The earlier Python CSV export returned 436 keys and silently merged some malformed quoted records; the game's own line/regex parser returns 605 keys including the correct current description. tools/inspect_windows_localization.py now follows those native parser semantics. Remove the unnecessary mod override. Runs45/46 remain diagnostic evidence of the wrong-key/custom-override investigation, not a shipped-game tooltip defect. Holy Spear and Tome of Might maximum-HP descriptions remain confirmed in the corrected export and independently reproduced in native damage tests.

- A48 — Existing content also has explicit numeric tuning outside the default pool. Android reduces GrenadeLauncherData damage 60 to 45, Abby starting HP 4 to 3, and Flash Mag evolution shots-to-trigger 5 to 20; its Shana CharacterData adds movement 3.2 / shooting movement factor 0.45 versus the shared Windows base movement.  The 33 existing evolution recipes normalize to the same graph/stat/gun contract except Flash Mag after accounting for managed-reference null IDs and damage categories. This is recipe comparison, not native method equivalence.
- A49 — Android Tome of Might appears to lose the current-HP scaling present on Windows. Windows AddHPToDamageMod multiplies current HP by 2. Android TomeOfMightPowerup.OnTweakDamge (0x18a3354) constructs an AddValueModifier from its serialized Damage=2 field directly; the inspected Apply (0x18a31e4) does not multiply it by player HP.  This is a static source anomaly, not a demonstrated Android runtime bug or a patch to Windows.
- A50 — Existing character exclusives add hidden global bonuses in the Android rewrite. Enhanced Shadow adds +25% SummonDamage in addition to setting ShadowClone cooldown multiplier 0.33; Windows applies its native +2 attackSpeedMod only. Necromancer adds +20% SummonDamage with its additional spirit producer; Windows attaches only the producer.  Exact profile memberships and fields are in extended-gameplay-comparison.json.
- A51 — Abby Whirlwind changes overlapping buffs to refresh. Windows attaches independent +35% fire/move TemporaryStatBuff instances for two seconds per reload. Android PF_BuffOnReload uses TemporaryRefreshingBuff.Activate (0x1976224) to reset the active timer, adding no second bonus while active.  Lilith Necrophile already uses the native refreshing helper; its matching helper name does not imply all reload buffs were already refreshing.
- A52 — Hastur exclusive modifiers move from mutating currently loaded native summons to keyed stat providers. Windows Smash/Frenzy enumerate loaded scene roots including inactive summons and add +30 base damage / +1 attack-speed modifier to Tentacle. Android registers Tentacle_damage / Tentacle_cd with StatsHolder.AddPower (0x1860d70/0x1860e0c).  Dasher's serialized killThreshold=25 is unused by its empty OnDeath (0x18a44b4); the inspected Update still uses time to transform, so that field alone is not a kill-based new feature.

A49 Windows remediation verified: the Windows Tome of Might tooltip also explicitly says maximum HP, while its AddHPToDamageMod used current HP. Native run47 reproduces the defect through the real first Forest boss drop, physics pickup and tome menu: 30 at 5/5, 28 at 4/5, 30 at 5/6. The shared scoped maximum-health modifier now handles only Holy Spear and Tome of Might; other current-HP perks remain unchanged. Run48 verifies 30 at 4/5, 32 at 5/6 and 6/6, then 48 after two native tome choices with seven maximum hearts even after injury, and clean original restart. The second tome uses a fixture pickup notification through the same native menu. Damage checks invoke the actual native enemy Health.TakeDamage pipeline; they do not claim projectile flight or Android runtime verification.
- A53 — Tome of Electro changes target count. Windows last ammo triggers all Enemy-layer colliders inside radius six for 22 base lightning damage. Android TomeOfElectroPowerup.OnAmmoChanged (0x18a2cd4) selects a single random enemy through its screen-related query and calls GenerateAt(22).
- A54 — Hina Hidden Blade changes both damage and protection. Windows damageMultiplier=3 matches its tooltip and computes floor(3*gun.damage). Android DamageOnShadowCloneDash.OnShadowCloneStart (0x18a3e2c) copies floor(gun.damage) and flips invulnerability; its end callback starts a delayed release.

- A55 — Body and Soul changes its existing timed pickup to direct shield grants in Android. Original Windows rune hierarchy includes both SoulAttunedRune (rank*1 capacity) and DropPrefabOverTime (PF_SoulHeartPickup every300 seconds); the earlier capacity-only conclusion omitted the second component. Android SoulAttunedRune.Init (0x1854dd0) instead adds source limitNums=3 plus shpMaxPerLevel*rank to capacity, schedules DropHP every300 seconds, and permits that many direct +1 shield grants (0x1854e68).  No absent timed feature is established; the source counter name DropHP does not mean a health pickup.
- A56 — Seismic Ward ranks appear to increase recovery time on Windows. The native tooltip says one second faster per rank; base native ward cooldown is10. Rune Init adds positive cdrPerLevel*rank as a flat attackSpeedMod; finalAttackCooldown adds that flat value. Android Init instead assigns baseCooldown10 minus rank. Run50 reproduces native11/15-second cooldowns at ranks1/5 and actual pulse spacing. RuneContracts.cs negates only the two native AddFlatBonus arguments, preserving native summoning and modifier composition; run51 verifies9/5-second cooldowns, real pulse intervals and clean original restart. The direct Android base-cooldown assignment still requires reconciliation with the earlier Windows sign correction.
- A57 — Growth's native current tooltip promises4% XP per rank, while Windows GrowthRuneData supplies0.02; Android supplies0.04. The broader Android XP curve remains required in the mode integration. Run50 reproduces2%/10% bonuses at ranks1/5. RuneContracts.cs corrects only the runtime Growth component's per-rank value to0.04; run51 verifies4%/20%, actual native XP gain, unchanged starting level requirement and clean original restart. Currency costs and all other Growth callbacks remain native.

- A58 — Elemental Barrage's producer duplicates Fire Rate instead of supplying Reload Rate. Both Windows ActivateBonus and Android0x1850ca8 add bonusStatMulti=.5 to FireRate twice; matching removal at0x1850d10 removes both. The native current tooltip promises50% Fire Rate and Reload Rate.
- A59 — Corrected source-reading false positive. Android OnSummonChanged at0x18500fc is a tail branch to CheckSummons at0x1850100, not an empty return. Fresh disassembly from the hash-verified supplied ARM64 binary confirms this. Both versions count active Summon objects, activate at exactly one, and add/remove0.1*rank SummonDamage/SummonAttackSpeed; their Init/observer/teardown paths match at this reviewed boundary. The prior recommendation to preserve a supposedly missing callback was based on an incorrect reading; no callback port is needed.
- A60 — Android Healing/Ethereal death counters omit Windows' Enemy-tag check. Their OnDeath0x18506dc/0x185171c accept valid Health senders and increment without tag qualification. Thresholds and Ethereal duration remain unchanged.
- A61 — Some rune tooltip mismatches are shared by both platforms. Cadence adds20%*rank size and damage, plus99piercing on every third shot; its current tooltip promises double size independently of rank. Tenacity data supplies3% FireRate and5% Knockback per rank while its tooltip says3% for both.
- A62 — Gun Glyph changes flat-damage composition and knockback. Native bullets use gun recipe damage times SummonStat.Modify(.1) and10% knockback. Android ShootBullets0x19a0204 instead applies SummonStat to recipe.damage then multiplies by.1, and retains recipe knockback. ProjectileRecipe.damage/size/knockback offsets18/20/24 and StatMod fields18/1c/20 establish the operands.
- A63 — Some rune collision filters move between callbacks and shared engine layers. Android Close Combat tracks any colliding Health without Windows' Enemy-tag condition; Wind Shield explicitly filters the enemy-projectile layer and releases its pooled projectile before cooldown. These source filters and projectile ownership are now integrated through native Windows collisions/pooling; S4/run-79/80 verifies actual collision delivery, damage, blocking and cooldown continuation. This supersedes the earlier instruction to retain Windows filters.
- A64 — Android dodge loses the native hit-counter guarantee. Its PlayerCollider.OnCollisionEnter2D0x18583dc computes Stat(Dodge).Modify(1)-1, clamps to Stat(DodgeCapMod).Modify(60)/100, and compares a random roll. Windows DodgeRoller additionally notifies TweakDodgeNotification and forces a dodge when its roll counter reaches1/chance, resetting that counter. That earlier endorsement of retaining the native guarantee is superseded: the current AndroidPlayerCollision adapter uses the source single capped random roll, without Windows' hit-counter guarantee or TweakDodgeNotification. Equal nominal percentages do not establish equal behavior. This also closes the application-only stat review's explicit dodge-consumer caveat.
- A65 — Windows boss arenas outlive an early-defeated boss until their60-second lifetime expires. Android BossSpawner.CreateArena0x196f268 binds its boss Health to LifeBinding.Update0x1965dec, which releases the arena after that owner dies. Native run62 reproduces the surviving barrier after actual boss damage death. BossArenaLifetime.cs adds the same missing owner at the exact native creation site, using verified Health local2; native onDeath provides deterministic cleanup without per-frame searches/polling. Run63 verifies early cleanup, independent overlapping boss arenas, active-arena scene exit and original Revolver restart. Native positions, collider/art and60-second expiry remain unchanged. The source method ranges and original native coroutine IL are retained; the patch rejects an unsupported local/creation pattern.
- A66 — Standard-map tuning differs beyond perk numbers. Android Forest changes several later-wave HP/population values and lowers first/second boss HP2500/35000 to2000/30000. Temple changes late squid timing/duration and cultist population, removing one late squid wave.  map-comparison.json retains every original MapData location and full wave/boss data, with tag-prefix labels normalized only for comparison and no positional pairing of missing waves.
- A67 — Android Endless rewrites mixed enemy pools, waves,60-second scaling curves, boss frequency/health and affixes. Its serialized map XP curve alone does not establish an executed XP consumer; A86 records the proven global XP formula. The current local implementation includes source schedules/growth/bosses and affix contacts, but remains unverified. The BulletDamageReduce affix mutates the stored Harmful integer by division by2, so a later contact can inherit already-halved damage; fire/targeted-lightning/freeze immunities follow their respective source consumers. No generic damage-reduction replacement is substituted. Pool limits/lifecycle are recorded under A87–A90. Source, offer and checkpoint coverage plus final integrated acceptance remain open.
- A68 — Pumpkin Patch uses distinct EyeBat/EyaBat pool tags. Original scene registration confirms both point to PF_EyeBat; the earlier possible-typo/missing-reference interpretation is superseded. Preserve the exact source tags and schedule. The complete source schedule is implemented locally, unverified; see the current-findings table above.
- A69 — Quickplay's opening BrainMonster wave has isElite=true on Windows and false on Android. Native isElite selects elite health/damage/speed modifiers and a different initial pool size; it does not itself add chest loot.

- A70 — Native Windows Endless briefly starts its displayed timer below zero. MapInitializer's Endless branch does not assign GameTimer.timeLimit, which remains-1; GameTimer.Update's first one-second-warning branch therefore sets timer=-2 before normal elapsed counting continues. Native S4/run-23 and run-24 checkpoints record approximately-1.998 after the fixture stops the actual clock. This is a proven existing native lifecycle issue, not evidence that the gameplay waves begin at a negative time: their WaitForSeconds schedules use scaled Unity time independently.  Resume records the actual native round's elapsed scaled time and explicit Endless cycle independently of the displayed game clock, retaining that clock and warning history without shifting wave/boss deadlines or applying Android's A67 balance rewrite.

- A71 — Resolved continuation integration hazard: a valid checkpoint can precede the native starting Blessing shield coroutine. Unconditionally executing its bootstrap on Continue creates an effect owner absent from the saved build (S4 diagnostic50, preserved early40 checkpoint). The port now observes the actual native wait and records pending/remaining state; reconstruction defers that body until its exact saved remaining delay. Native immediate-save51/fresh-process52 verify no early shield, one grant and one offer consumption; completed-bootstrap Custom55/56 also passes. This is a correction to the unpublished port, not a claim of a shipped Windows save bug or an Android balance improvement. No guessed delay or general object serialization.

- A72 — Continuation applied a dependent shield upgrade before the reconstructed shield's native Start had initialized its cooldown StatMod. The new Android Stalwart action exposed the missing lifecycle boundary in S4/run-67/68; bounded probes identify the actual action and valid source prefab, rather than a missing content key. Replay now waits on the native shield's non-null cooldown owner before processing later acquisitions. S4/run-66 saves the active Android refreshing-effects/shield build; run-69 restores it in a fresh process, verifies exact owners/contributions/recovery, expiry, reactivation and original restart. No deadline, arbitrary sleep or user save replacement is introduced.

Current parity checkpoint: A02/A03/A05 and the five Tome numeric recipes from A04/A48 are integrated at the native recipe/application boundary (S3/run-74). A17/A42 cooldown/recovery arithmetic passes run-77; broader shield owner/repeat reconciliation remains open. A25/A51 refreshing helpers pass run-79 and S4/run-66/69 continuation. Historical retention instructions above remain superseded; all other required Android gameplay differences remain open until verified.

P2 burn/health checkpoint: S3/run-80 reproduces independent Windows burn decay; run-81 verifies Android shared accumulation/refreshed five-tick duration/1000 cap, death cleanup and restart. - A73 — Android Tome of Might has a signed-damage anomaly beyond A49's missing HP scaling. The shipped Damage field is positive2. OnTweakDamge0x18a3354 adds AddValueModifier(0,2), whose Modify0x19271f4 adds to the current value. Android Health.HPChange0x1979574 passes the signed negative change through IntExtensions.NotifyModifiers0x1927774 without taking an absolute value; e.g. -20 becomes -18. Windows modifiers receive positive damage, so the source-equivalent modifier subtracts2 there. S4/run-72 verifies native raw20 becomes18 for both Bullet and Thunder independently of HP. This supersedes the earlier positive-addition interpretation in A49; static Android source-path evidence and reproduced Windows behavior do not constitute Android device execution. The source signed behavior is implemented and verified.

P2 health-provider checkpoint: S4/run-72 verifies real menu acquisition of Death Rounds' flat Bullet/Summon stat contributions, Bloodsuckers/Soul Drain direct grants on the five-hundredth native Summon kill (and rejection of a Bullet kill), Soul Conversion capacity-then-grant, signed Tome of Might behavior and two Elements tomes' additive elemental modifiers/compounded bullet penalties. Counters317 checkpoint through Save & return; fresh-process run-73 restores both counters and elemental providers once, then verifies clean original restart/firing. Run-70 cancelled a fixture waiting for a disabled horde's BrainMonster; the replacement uses an actual native enemy-pool prefab. Its initial trigger prototype also assumed typed DeathEvent payloads; inspection identified Windows' existing Health.SummonDamageKill channel, now used directly. Run-71 is diagnostic: it passes grants/checkpoint but tests an incorrect positive-damage interpretation before the signed pipeline was traced. Remaining damage/health consumers, summon exclusives, rune rules, maps/Endless and coverage block P2 completion/release.

No Windows preference or review gate applies to remaining differences.

- A74 — Signed damage rounding crosses different native API boundaries. Android Health.HPChange0x1979574 passes signed changes through IntExtensions.NotifyModifiers0x1927774, which floors each modifier stage. Windows Health.TakeDamage supplies positive damage, requiring a ceiling at each corresponding stage; direct HPChange stays signed and uses a floor. Close Combat's rank-three 9% boost therefore turns raw20 into22, rather than21. Tome of Might must subtract two in positive damage notifications but add two for signed HPChange, including positive healing. Scoped notification context preserves both call paths and nested dispatch; S4/run-81/82 verifies acquisition and fresh-process Continue for damage through each API, plus positive HP changes and clean original restart.

P2 rune-consumer checkpoint: S4/run-79/80 verifies A55 direct five-minute soul grants, capacity and finite count; A60 unqualified native Health death counters; A63 real Close Combat/Wind Shield collisions, pooled projectile release and native cooldown; and A35 Regeneration's ninety-second first pickup. Fresh-process Continue restores Body and Soul's remaining interval/count plus active Ethereal/Wind Shield clocks, observes expiry/recovery and verifies no owner/capacity/ammo carry into an original restart. Run-78 used an invalid net-active-pickup assertion; the corrected fixture checks the actual distant kill/drop position and native counter without a production workaround. These consumers are integrated; other gameplay, map/Endless, coverage and final delivery requirements still block P2 completion. No release or deployment.

- A75 — Hellspawns/Thunderspawns listen to typed surviving-health notifications, not summon-producer hits. Android Health.HPChange0x1979574 posts OnHurtEvent with EnumDamage after hurt/heal dispatch only when the target remains alive; invulnerability/negative damage to an already-dead actor exits earlier. Hellspawns0x18a22cc filters OnSummon and BossMonster; Thunderspawns0x18a2548 filters OnSummon and rolls below0.15 without that boss exclusion. Native SummonOnHitTrigger instead listens to producers and can announce protected/lethal attempts while missing other Summon-classified attacks. The typed surviving native damage bridge and source callbacks are integrated. Actual boss-spawner identity is retained on the pooled actor independently of Endless checkpoint-list resets; the Champion tag alone is not the source boss discriminator.

- A76 — The shared lightning overloads differ beyond A23's perk callback. Android targeted GenerateAt0x19a3a98 enables the PF_Thunder Harmful child and assigns floored damageMod-adjusted damage; the shipped child uses EnumDamage.OnPlayer=1 (Bullet classification), a0.35 circle and source collision delivery. Harmful.OnTrigger0x197d7d4 passes that category to Health.HPChange; it is not the positional overload's Thurnder=7 category. Positional GenerateAt0x19a476c disables that child behavior, queries monster-pool centers at baseAoE*sizeMultiplier, applies its signed input and posts ThunderHitEvent per eligible actor. Windows delegates its targeted overload to an immediate Physics2D area query, applies the shared damage modifier there and posts one target notification afterward; its positional overload posts none. These are required shared-backend migration/integration gaps, including the A67 affix consumers. Original-pointer prefab evidence is retained in the private source analysis; matching names or the successful proc-callback checks do not establish backend parity.

A76 sign/physics follow-through: the reachable Android Flash Mag LineThunderAttackTowadsCursorAction coroutine0x185fe1c supplies positive22 to the positional overload at0x185fed4/0x185ff20/0x185ff6c; that overload passes its input to HPChange without negation or damageMod application. This yields a positive HP change in the inspected path. The pooled Harmful child is a kinematic/full-contact Rigidbody2D and0.35 non-trigger circle; disabling its MonoBehaviour does not disable the collider or erase its stored damage. Both [Unity2019.4](https://docs.unity3d.com/2019.4/Documentation/ScriptReference/MonoBehaviour.OnCollisionEnter2D.html) and [Unity2022.3](https://docs.unity3d.com/2022.3/Documentation/ScriptReference/MonoBehaviour.OnCollisionEnter2D.html) document collision callbacks to disabled behaviours. Source-equivalent reconstruction must preserve the signed immediate change and actual collision/pool state, then verify their combined outcome. These are required implementation details, not a balance decision or Android-device execution claim.

A76 pool follow-through: GameObjectPool<object>.Get0x252b66c pops from its inactive stack; Release0x252bb28 pushes onto it. Windows ObjectPooler rotates through inactive entries. Because a positional cast retains the selected Harmful component's captured damage, the selection order is gameplay-relevant. The lightning adapter now follows source last-released-first order while preserving native object ownership and TimeToLive deactivation. Run89's failed fixture expectation exposed this previously unported source difference; it was resolved in the implementation rather than weakening the expected source behavior.

A79 — Holy Shield ownership and Holy Arts notification integration. Android HolyShield attaches another complete PF_HolyShield per reached application. Each source PreventDamage owner contributes one flip to counted protection, decrements its remaining hit count on every prevention notification and keeps protecting/active during the specified0.5-second break. It then unflips its contribution and schedules GameTimer+max(10,base-reduction); recharge requires strictly greater game time. Each shield contribution belongs to Health.isInvincible itself; the break does not add a separate transient invulnerability flip. A121 corrects the earlier independent native isProtected adapter and its inaccurate field description. Stalwart and Sword & Shield affect only the first found shield. Repeated Divine Blessing/Wrath helpers also bind to that first shield; Wrath uses strict9x5 pooled monster centers. A01's earlier one-shield/rate-growth result is superseded for this recipe, while its generic repeated-key identity correction remains.

The retained native SmiteCR reproduces its query/effects/timing; its health call is adapted to signed Smite-category damage. Source SmiteKillNotification is posted after every captured collider, including surviving targets. That unusual behavior is required parity and drives Sword & Shield on hits; it is not postponed for balance review. ## Positioned impact and burning-field pool consumers

A164 — Integration issue (I), not an Android bug. SpawnOnCollision.OnTrigger0x1975f3c supplies the target/projectile midpoint to GetPooledObject, stores the returned effect and assigns that midpoint again when nonnull. Its source ordinary collision wrapper supplies no Health and returns without an effect; the projectile sampler provides the actual contact. The local sampler now uses the positioned source pool overload, preserving this order rather than acquiring at zero and separately activating after movement.

PeriodicallyBurnArea.Start0x197b630 caches BurnSystem.SharedInstance. Update0x197b694 uses one reusable ID list, a strict greater-than timer and one interval subtraction, source GetMonsterInRange, then ID resolution/Burn. The earlier port scanned the complete native pool lists and applied different active/layer/Health filters. The local burning field now uses the source active-monster query, including its existing visibility membership semantics. The old A13 truncation fix remains historical; its earlier native-buffer/collider description does not describe this new query.

ObjectPoolItem’s amountToVis/createMax/initCount fields are already consumed through the source scene table and AndroidPools visible/maximum/initial values; the native item object remains the Windows public-list bridge. No extra pool framework or behavior is introduced. A165 — Source lifecycle quirk/integration issue (Q/I). ExplosiveProjectile.Start0x198d740 and OnDestroy0x198d744 return immediately. OnDisable0x198dba8 calls SpawnExplosive without Windows’ initialized/scene/quit conditions. The Windows class instead registers its explosion prefab per projectile and installs an activeSceneChanged handler; its local suppression therefore changes the source callback. No Android teardown failure is asserted from these static differences, and no new suppression/guard is introduced.

The local adapter now uses the source empty lifecycle and unconditional disable spawn. All three Android grenade variants use GrenadeExplosion; the source scene table owns that tag with maximum99999, initial0 and no visibility limit. The native asset is named PF_GrenadeExplosion, so that exact owned prefab is mapped to the source tag/data. Spawn keeps Android’s unpositioned global acquisition, integer damage assignment, position/scale/SetActive order, optional shake/sound and primary-only explosion notification. It does not borrow A164’s positioned overload for this different source caller. Existing typed contact configuration serves the original explosion; the obsolete extra marker/transpiler is removed.

## World-space pickup movement and XP initialization

A167 — Gameplay/integration difference (G/I). Android Pickup0x18573a8..0x1857948 uses cached collider/trail/target fields and a0.3-second frame-driven world-space Lerp toward the live target. It does not reparent to the player or use the Windows LeanTween coroutine. TargetPick rejects null/already-owned targets; the collision callback ignores its collider argument and selects the current player. Update enables the existing circle collider at strict XY distance<15, then advances movement. Arrival invokes the actual virtual UsePickup, optional sound, pool release and target clear. OnDisable clears only the target; next Init hides the trail. Original HP and both XP asset values are0.30000001192092896. The existing shared pool now installs the companion on new HP/XP counterparts, leaving Windows-only SHPPickup on its native path.

SmallXP’s original spt pointer binds Bounce’s SpriteRenderer. XPPickup.Init0x1857bcc calls base Init then sets that renderer enabled, so reuse reverses its prior hidden state. The native Bounce renderer is the owned counterpart. This refines A117: the generic pool does not restore hidden renderers, but this source consumer does. Native/source XPPickup.UsePickup share root PlayerXP lookup, optional amount grant and unconditional pickup notification. Its compiled OnVis/Max declarations do not prove a callback registration; actual producer/reachability remains a G2 requirement and no extra visibility pipeline is invented.

A168 — Conditional source defect/unresolved reachability (B/U). The original LargeXP15 raw spt PPtr is exactly file0/path0, while the same Init unconditionally assigns spt.enabled. This is not an unresolved pointer or missing native graphics: both original hierarchies have renderer children, but the Android field is null. If this source initialization is reached, that assignment dereferences null. The adapter preserves the original null and source branch; it does not choose a renderer, catch the exception or add a guard. The source scene table registers LargeXP; no other LargeXP literal appears in the decoded gameplay rows. That bounded inventory result does not prove no executable producer or establish an Android runtime exception. Actual producer/reachability and any runtime renderer assignment before Init remain open; no confirmed runtime symptom or developer intent is asserted.

No source bytes were patched and no game/test/build ran. Current pickup movement/radius/reuse/arrival/native/Custom/current/legacy integration and whole-port continuation remain unverified P2 work. ## Start-skill accept and automatic XP collection producer

A171 — Gameplay/quirk/integration gap with unresolved entry (G/Q/I/U). The decoded start-skill accept branch calls AutoPickXpSystem.LevelUp before applying each selected powerup; the close-only route is separate. LevelUp gives60+10×previous level seconds and advances its level. While active, fixed updates decrement duration and pulse Effective at an accumulated0.8 seconds, resetting only that pulse accumulator. LevelUp does not clear its existing partial accumulator, and a last decrement can still pulse before duration is clamped to zero. Pause()0x1871934 writes Time.timeScale0 then true; UnPause()0x1871cc4 writes1 then false. Windows uses a nested BoolToggle counter and conditionally restores a stored time scale. Native Windows-only SetTimeScale remains available to its existing callers; source UnPause still assigns1 unconditionally.

One UnPause therefore clears source pause state even after multiple Pause calls; timed completion can likewise unpause another owner. These are direct assignment consequences. Actual original menu/timed overlap reachability or an Android runtime symptom is not established, so this is not classified as a confirmed source bug or independently corrected. ### A180–A183 — burn ownership and source exit release

A released state popped for another target can inherit a partial tick phase. This differs from ordinary duration tuning because an otherwise-reset object's previous target influences its next use. The source state is retained; no elapsed reset or independent fix was added. Actual carried-phase gameplay has not been run.

A182 — Likely Android bug / quirk with unobserved preconditions (B/Q/U). UpdateLogic0x19251c4 returns immediately for count<1 without releasing. ### A223 — source low-FPS damage display

A223 — Gameplay/quirk/integration finding (G/Q/I). Source GameController.Update0x1870464 increments a static frame counter and samples only when lastInterval+updateInterval is strictly below real time. Calculation uses frames divided by current float time minus float.Parse(lastInterval.ToString()), then clears frames and stores the current time. If the existing ShowDamagePopup(default1) is enabled and0<fps<20, add interval1 to the instance low timer; after8 set the once flag, reset timer, write preference0 and show the tip. Other samples/settings reset the timer. This accumulates qualifying sample intervals, rather than measuring exact wall-clock time since the first low sample. It writes no PlayerPrefs.Save and cannot auto-disable again on that controller after the user re-enables the setting. The adapter now reproduces that actual reached rule; it is not an independently invented performance safeguard.

### A232 — Culling and elemental summon callbacks belong to original assets

A232 — Gameplay/integration/quirk finding (G/I/Q). Culling3602 applies its source+30% stat, then registers its own Health.OnHurt callback. Matching Summon damage on a surviving target skips exactly CreatureType2, rolls strictly below15%, then applies HPChange(-999999,Powerup11). This is ordinary signed damage with modifiers, protection, feedback and typed death, rather than AutoKill. It can fail to kill a sufficiently large/protected target and does not supply a Summon-classified kill. These source consequences are retained without a balance correction.

Hellspawns3604 and Thunderspawns3605 also register one callback on their original asset. Hellspawns filters Summon/type!=2 and burns3. Thunderspawns checks Summon then rolls15% before resolving Health, generating lightning22 without a creature filter. All three still apply their stats on repeat. Equal delegates mean one callback per source asset, rather than one per pick. Their source classes have no destroy/deactivate override removing these callbacks; existing source notification clear owns scene cleanup.

The port previously supplied only Culling's stat recipe and retained native summon-hit AutoKill. Its two elemental adapters used per-pick listeners and approximated BossMonster with the boss-spawn path. Original Health fields contradict that proxy: Elder Brain, Winged Monster and Spawner Monster are2, while Rhogog, Walking Tree, Spawned Bug and Tentacle Enemy are0. Zero is not equivalent to the source IsPlayer test. All32 mapped native health recipe names now carry their original consistent creature field, and the unused spawn-identity classification path is removed.

Original-profile-owned callback assets now route those actual typed events into existing signed Health, burn and lightning consumers. Native split PlayerHealth/Health is bridged explicitly. Native stat/Custom scaling remains, and known old native/earlier per-pick element effect identities translate to the new original-asset registration. Unknown static aggregates remain the separate A230 task; no value is guessed. No extra chance counter, safety filter or static owner cache is added.

### A235 — sound-pool exhaustion affects gameplay RNG and shares the fixed clock

A235 — Gameplay/integration/quirk finding (G/I/Q). Native SoundEffectSO.Play limits ten concurrent sounds per asset name and creates/destroys Sound objects. Android uses the shared Sound pool with visible-1/max250/initial0; its existing count>max comparison permits251 active pooled sources. A failed pool acquisition returns before clip, volume and pitch random draws. This changes subsequent proc-roll ordering, so treating this body as presentation alone omitted a gameplay consumer.

The Battle path now uses the source pool and default2D Audio Source template, whose original clip/mixer are null and playOnAwake is true. Source/native common sound fields and the clip chooser match; native clips/settings/title presentation remain under M1. Source playback chooses the clip, draws volume and pitch/semitones, plays, then schedules floor(clip.length*1000/pitch). Its empty-clip return and catch(System.Exception) returning the current AudioSource are retained. No new catch/fallback/volume clamp is invented.

Source TimeOutRelease adds the duration to static CDData.time. Duplicate keys increment by one millisecond until unused. ObjectPooler.Update collects due keys, releases their targets, then removes the keys. ReleaseAll does not clear pending deadlines; no reuse/stale-release correction is added. The source generic release disables an active unpooled foreign-name target, matching the existing unowned boundary.

The actual clock writer is GameTimer.FixedUpdate. It independently adds floor(fixedDeltaTime*1000) to the integer CD clock while enabled, and fixedDeltaTime to the playing run timer. Constructor flags start true; Awake resets the float timer, not the static CD clock. Source warning/limit assignment and callback order replace native rendered-frame accumulation and its Endless guard. Existing source map deadlines remain. Start enables both clocks; Stop(false) disables both. KillEnemies/PlayerDead/Survived use false, but the reached Forest Rhogog caller uses Stop(true). Its current prefix now retains the CD clock during that encounter. The clock is not a new wall-time monitor or saved history.

ChallengeBattleUI.OnEnemyDeath0x1886b74 accepts every non-player Health; IsBoss0x1886d40 compares CreatureType with2. OnEnable/OnDisable own its listener. Counters still update after completion, while CheckChallengeComplete0x1886dc0 publishes ChallengeMode.Complete only once. RestoreKills0x1886e8c resets the completed bit and checks the restored values immediately. The native quota owner now follows those boundaries; CombatState.Enter/Exit register/remove the completion adapter, matching the original source listener lifetime. No Enemy-tag, prefab-name, pause/combat death filter or periodic completion retry is retained. Source completion outside an active Combat listener is not repaired by a new delivery queue.

### A254 — shared Kunoichi retained-clock limit branch

A254 — Quirk/unresolved/no-new-defect finding (Q/U/N). Both original FireRateAndPierceOnNotHurt.Update implementations add deltaTime only while ticks<maxTicks. Their subsequent timer>=secsPerTick branch is outside that condition: one interval is subtracted and one tick grants fireRateBoostPerTick/+pierceBoostPerTick even if ticks already reached the configured limit. With a sufficiently large retained clock at the limit, subsequent Updates can therefore add more ticks without accumulating new time. This is a conditional source calculation, not an observed Android symptom; ordinary shipped timing reachability and intent are unestablished.

Original fields match: five ticks, ten seconds,0.1 fire rate/+1 pierce per tick. The actual native attachment and owned-player Start/destruction match source behavior, with the source unused typed hurt argument mapped to the native hurt callback. Injury reverses all counted contributions and resets timer/ticks. Source does not save this helper phase; ordered current/legacy replay reconstructs it under the chosen Android rebuild policy. The matching branch remains unchanged, with no extra cap, loop or phase import. The full ARM/CIL evidence is retained with A253. Actual gameplay/Continue acceptance remains pending with the whole port; unknown intent alone does not block preserving established behavior.

### A256 — inherited buff save roots and unsaved derived lifecycle

A256 — Likely-bug/quirk/integration finding (B/Q/I), extending the existing A143 mechanism. Android AttachPrefabPowerup.TryGetInGameSaveRuntimeValues calls GetComponentInChildren<BuffPlayerStats>(true), which includes subclasses, and emits positive saved counts. Its reader obtains the inherited component and calls RestoreInGameSaveStacks. The original attachment/type inventory contains seven such roots: Generator, Berserk, Bulk Up, Pyromaniac, Strong Will's NonStackingBuff, and Whirlwind/Necrophile's TemporaryRefreshingBuff. The port's source-owner map only contained the four direct-base roots and its exact-type provider lookup therefore missed the other three.

Capture, validation, grouped source replay and deferred first Start now include all seven actual roots with their correct runtime types and source root names. Whirlwind's reconstructed root is PF_BuffOnReload. Family/list order and source omission of zero counts remain. The existing source counter consumer applies the recorded counts; it does not persist or synthesize NonStackingBuff's active flag or TemporaryRefreshingBuff's old coroutine/timer.

Source ApplyBuff increments the saved count while RemoveBuff does not decrement it (A143). A temporary or conditional helper can therefore retain a positive count after its contribution was removed. Restoring that count into a fresh helper can apply contributions without the old expiry coroutine or active flag; subsequent threshold activation can combine with restored contributions. This is a source-confirmed conditional lifecycle mismatch, not an observed Android-device reproduction or a new independent mechanism. Preserve it rather than cap counts, set active flags or reinstall unsaved phases. A143/A256 count as one likely-bug family.

Legacy exact-type providers did not record inherited stacks: NonStackingBuff has no old stack provider, while TemporaryRefreshingBuff's old provider contains only its timer. The user-selected Android rebuild retains known four direct-base provider ordinals and does not infer missing inherited counts from raw stats, partial timers or old temporary effect lists. Earlier incomplete schema7 records similarly cannot recover counts that were never written. Original checkpoint files remain untouched on read; subsequent source captures include all seven roots.

The private A255/A256 contract includes both full original attachment save/read dispatches, seven original root/type records, ten prior base-buff source ranges and current implementation hashes. Source integrated, uncompiled/unverified. Actual positive/sparse/repeated count replay, fresh-process re-save/Continue, subsequent threshold/reload/death events, known legacy ordinal retention and Custom/whole-port delivery remain required after remaining source implementation and final anomaly review. P2 remains sole ACTIVE; no build/test/player run, deployment or release.

A259 — Gameplay/quirk/integration/no-new-defect finding (G/Q/I/N). Android Gun.StartShooting immediately sets its shooting flag; Windows starts a separate wait coroutine. The source Update gates pause/shootEnable before subtracting its positive timer, consumes at most one shot per frame and creates a fresh recipe per shooter. It publishes ShootEvent before ordinary shots only when fireOnStop is false. Source StopShooting clears the shooting flag, computes direction and a fresh recipe, publishes that event only for release-fired shooters, then invokes their virtual release callback. Windows instead publishes during charging and omits the release publication, disconnecting next-shot recipe modifiers from the actual charged release. AndroidShooting.cs now owns this connected flow behind the actual native Gun entry points, using the existing source recipe/shooter/pool/ammo consumers and Windows cursor projection. ID211 Sword alone receives the source per-shot Attack animation trigger; native scalar/cursor/OnShoot and explicit skin/presentation boundaries remain.

Source release also plays the gun sound only when the first shooter is not release-fired, opposite the native condition. Ordinary release can therefore play a sound without an additional shot at this boundary. This is preserved as Q; the evidence does not justify an independent audio fix or claiming all charged-shooter audio is absent. Source shootEnable is retained as its own BoolToggle, initially true, without an invented disabling producer.

The complete ShootingState entry/exit/wait/re-fire flow now lives beside the native state and replaces its real entry/exit calls. Pending transitions remove cancel/reload/ammo handlers, install re-fire handlers, wait for shotReady and then change state. Re-fire with ammo stops only the stored iterator, restores handlers and removes its re-fire listeners. Android's zero-ammo callback starts an untracked wait without Windows' stored-iterator assignment or coalescing guard. Its stored canceled iterator is not cleared by re-fire. These quirks are retained; native Aim release/performed events map the mobile joystick callbacks. Source cancel always selects Reload when ammo is empty or manual reload is available, otherwise Idle. The existing localized Windows Auto Reload control therefore shows On/read-only, while its stored Windows preference is preserved for use without the mod. Character shooting movement factors, disableAction and the already-owned reload flow use this same state.

Remaining XP leaves match once the existing source threshold getter is applied: modified XP, strict greater-than threshold, one level per call and level/threshold/XP notification order; level1/xp0/threshold15 startup; raw XP getter and current Continue restore order. Original GM level-up publication belongs to the developer menu, with version8.6/build1093 openGMUI/IsGM/development all false. No cheat menu is invented. Health FinalDeath only invokes onDeath and its aligned direct caller is the native-adapted final-death/results state; Rebirth adds maxHP with None damage and its direct caller is the ad/coupon/subscription revival state, excluded under M7. Direct-call attribution is bounded and does not claim universal indirect-call absence. All80 original serialized MapData curve fields use ease0 or34; existing source linear/circular-in consumers cover those actual configurations. Other compiled easing functions are not additional original gameplay features. PlayerXP itself does not consume the serialized PlayerXpDataUp field; no new XP curve producer is invented.

Private remaining-gun-state-source/source-contract.json binds complete gun/state/GM source ranges, XP/health and revival bodies/callers, original disabled GM build flags, native CIL, both native options bindings, all serialized curve fields and current source hashes. tools/reconcile_firing_flows.py reproduces the bounded record. Gun timer/charge/pending-input phases remain source-unsaved and reconstruct through current/rebuilt legacy Continue; Custom uses the same real gun/recipe flow. Source integrated, uncompiled/unverified. Remaining whole-port source reconciliation and final anomaly review precede actual ordinary/charged/modifier/ammo/re-fire/disable/options/Custom/current-legacy/PC/phone/package/release acceptance. P2 remains sole ACTIVE; no build, test, player run, deployment or release.

## Stillness movement semantics — 2026-10-26

User-approved bug correction: original Windows `sharedassets1.assets:729` targets `WalkSpeed−100%`, applied by the native shooting state; Android8.6 `sharedassets1.assets:3506` targets global `MoveSpeed−100%`. The port copied that Android recipe, preventing movement even outside shooting.1.1.6’s acceptance incorrectly treated global immobilization as intended. This is a confirmed gameplay-semantic defect, not an optional balance proposal. Restore the native WalkSpeed channel while keeping bullet damage/size+100% and projectiles+1; describe the shooting-only restriction explicitly. Current checkpoint acquisition replay rebuilds the corrected recipe, without a save reset or added compatibility mechanism. Native97 reproduces the fault before the correction: ordinary movement is zero immediately after the actual Tome pick. Corrected firing/release/reload, old1.1.6/current Continue, compact HUD and final release acceptance remain required until recorded below.

Stillness correction source acceptance: native98/105/106 verifies the actual pick, firing-only restriction, normal release/reload movement and original1.1.6/current checkpoint rebuild; currentrun-131/run-132 retains exact bonuses and corrected channel in the integrated build. The right-column UI refinement does not alter stat/state/replay owners. Final1.1.7 packaged runtime and independent public-delivery evidence are recorded in PROGRESS.md once delivered.

Stillness final delivery: published1.1.7 exact package verifies native fresh/current/original1.1.6 checkpoint firing/release/reload in S6/29/30/31/32/33/34/35/36/37. Independent public package and exact rollback receipts close the correction; see PROGRESS.md.

## Edge-marker coordinate conversion — 2026-10-26

Confirmed Revamped UI defect: the earlier boss pointer assigned a screen-to-parent-local point directly to a center-anchored marker, while its HUD parent uses a bottom-left pivot. This added the parent's center twice and could put the complete pointer outside the visible screen. The previous test checked only a minimum x coordinate and missed the upper bound. Native136 records a boss icon at x1284–1311 on a900-pixel display. The shared layout now subtracts the parent's actual rect center when assigning the anchored position. Complete icon, rotated arrow and distance bounds are checked, alongside shared-direction separation, safe-area fit and collection/scene cleanup. This is a correction to our UI, not an Android gameplay change. Current exploration acceptance and final delivery are tracked in PROGRESS.md.

Native154 exposed a collection lifecycle detail in the shared layout: opening the native reward menu disables the HUD root and its layout before the collected marker's active flag is cleared. The world-owned target now clears its marker on collection even while that HUD is disabled. Native reward state and pickup semantics remain unchanged.
